  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The boundary value problem y''=κ²yⁿ with y(0)=1, y(1)=0 arises in important physical applications such as radiative heat transfer and chemical reactions, but its solution behavior as a function of parameters needs careful qualitative and quantitative analysis.

Method: The authors employ upper/lower envelope techniques to study the qualitative and quantitative dependence of the solution on parameters, and reformulate the boundary value problem into an equivalent initial value problem that is straightforward to solve.

Result: The paper establishes qualitative and quantitative insights into how the solution depends on the parameters κ and n, and provides a simplified computational approach via transformation to an initial value problem.

Conclusion: The upper/lower envelope approach yields a clear understanding of parametric dependence of the solution, and the transformation to an initial value problem makes the problem practically solvable with ease.

Abstract: We consider the boundary value problem $y''=κ^2y^n, y(0)=1, y(1)=0,$ relevant to radiative heat transfer, chemical reactions, etc. We use upper/lower envelopes to study the qualitative and quantitative dependence of its solution on parameters, and transform it into an easily solvable initial value problem.

</details>


### [22] [Logarithmic Resonance at Mixed Boundary Junctions in Gradient-Dependent Semilinear Equationsv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01790)
*Marin Mišur*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper proves that gradient-dependent semilinear perturbations (linear in |∇u|) at mixed Dirichlet-Neumann junctions introduce an O(r^{-1/2}) source term that resonates with the half-integer spectrum of the principal operator, causing a logarithmic anomaly. The exact r^{3/2} ln(r) profile is derived with its coefficients for various ℓ_q-norm penalties, and an enriched XFEM space is shown to be quasi-optimal with numerical confirmation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While the linear behavior of solutions at mixed Dirichlet-Neumann boundary junctions (characterized by O(r^{1/2}) singular functions) is well documented, the effect of gradient-dependent semilinear perturbations on this local asymptotic profile has not been analyzed. Standard separable polynomial assumptions fail at O(r^{3/2}) order, and a rigorous analytical understanding of this obstruction is needed.

Method: The authors establish a generalized resonance theorem showing that linearly scaling gradient penalties (with |∇u|) induce a non-orthogonal resonance with the half-integer spectrum of the principal homogeneous differential operator. They derive the exact analytical r^{3/2} ln(r) profile, compute explicit logarithmic coefficients and angular offsets for ℓ_1, ℓ_2, ℓ_∞, and arbitrary ℓ_q-norm penalties, and formalize an enriched continuous Galerkin (XFEM) space with quasi-optimality guarantees.

Result: The paper proves the emergence of a logarithmic anomaly yielding an r^{3/2} ln(r) profile, provides rigorous local Sobolev regularity bounds for the remainder, and demonstrates universality of this obstruction across all ℓ_q-norm penalties. Finite element experiments confirm the predicted localized pollution and recover the predicted logarithmic coefficients on flat junctions, while resolving the junction recovers optimal degree-of-freedom efficiency.

Conclusion: The logarithmic anomaly is an inherent obstruction for standard finite element methods when gradient-dependent penalties are present at mixed boundary junctions. The enriched XFEM formulation resolves this issue quasi-optimally, and targeted software architectures are outlined for a fully enriched implementation.

Abstract: The regularity of solutions to elliptic partial differential equations degrades severely at mixed Dirichlet-Neumann boundary junctions, characterized classically by an $\mathcal{O}(r^{1/2})$ leading singular function. While this linear behavior is well documented, the introduction of gradient-dependent semilinear perturbations alters the local asymptotic profile. This article proves that gradient penalties scaling linearly with $|\nabla u|$ induce a highly localized $\mathcal{O}(r^{-1/2})$ source term that resonates with the half-integer spectrum of the principal homogeneous differential operator. This non-orthogonal resonance causes standard separable polynomial assumptions to fail at order $\mathcal{O}(r^{3/2})$. We establish a generalized resonance theorem that forces the emergence of a logarithmic anomaly, providing the exact analytical formulation of the resulting $r^{3/2} \ln(r)$ profile alongside rigorous local Sobolev regularity bounds for the remainder. By calculating the exact logarithmic coefficients and angular offsets for $\ell_1$, $\ell_2$, $\ell_\infty$, and arbitrary $\ell_q$-norm penalties, we demonstrate the universality of this obstruction. Finally, we formalize the corresponding enriched continuous Galerkin space (XFEM), establish its quasi-optimality, and present finite element experiments that confirm the predicted localized pollution, recover the predicted logarithmic coefficients across the $\ell_1$, $\ell_2$, and $\ell_\infty$ penalties on a curvature-free flat junction, and show that resolving the junction recovers optimal degree-of-freedom efficiency in standard finite element solvers; we close by outlining the targeted software architectures required for a fully enriched implementation.

</details>


### [23] [Numerical approximation of fractional diffusion equations on metric graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01932)
*David Bolin,Lenin Riera-Segura,Alexandre B. Simas*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper develops a rigorous mathematical framework and fully discrete numerical scheme for fractional diffusion equations on compact metric graphs, using backward Euler time-stepping, finite elements, and rational approximations of the fractional operator, with error estimates for all discretizations verified numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fractional diffusion equations on metric graphs are important for modeling nonlocal dynamics in network-like structures, but lack a rigorous numerical framework due to the complexity of fractional operators on such domains.

Method: The authors establish a mathematical framework for the shifted Kirchhoff-Laplacian fractional powers, then propose a fully discrete scheme combining backward Euler time-stepping with finite element spatial discretization. The fractional operator is approximated via rational approximations, converting the problem into a sequence of sparse elliptic solves.

Result: Error estimates are derived for temporal, spatial, and rational discretizations, and convergence is confirmed through numerical experiments.

Conclusion: The proposed scheme provides an efficient and convergent numerical method for fractional diffusion on compact metric graphs.

Abstract: We study fractional diffusion equations on compact metric graphs, where the nonlocal dynamics is governed by fractional powers of the shifted Kirchhoff-Laplacian. Building on recent advances in the analysis of fractional operators on metric graphs, we establish a rigorous mathematical framework and propose a fully discrete scheme based on backward Euler time-stepping and finite element discretization. To approximate the action of the fractional operator, we employ rational approximations, reducing the problem to a sequence of sparse elliptic solves for efficient implementation. We derive error estimates for the temporal, spatial, and rational discretizations, and confirm convergence through numerical experiments.

</details>


### [24] [An $H^{-1}$ least-squares UnCut FEM on domains defined by a level set function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02015)
*Jiashun Hu,Buyang Li,Han Yang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Proposes UnCut FEM, a new finite element method for Poisson and Stokes equations on curved domains represented by level sets, using a least-squares H^{-1} residual formulation that avoids numerical integration over cut subregions and eliminates user-tuned stabilization parameters, with rigorous optimal-order convergence proofs in the H^1 norm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cut finite element methods for domains with curved boundaries typically require numerical integration over cut subregions and large stabilization parameters that must be user-tuned, which is cumbersome and negatively impacts robustness. The authors aim to overcome these limitations.

Method: The UnCut FEM introduces a least-squares formulation that minimizes an H^{-1} residual of the governing equations. This avoids numerical integration over cut subregions of boundary elements (similar to φ-FEM) and ensures stability without large stabilization parameters, eliminating the need for penalty parameter tuning.

Result: Optimal-order convergence of UnCut FEM solutions is rigorously established in the H^1 norm for both Poisson and Stokes equations. Numerical experiments are presented to support the theoretical analysis.

Conclusion: The UnCut FEM provides a robust and parameter-free alternative to existing cut FEM approaches for curved-boundary problems, with proven optimal convergence for both Poisson and Stokes equations.

Abstract: We propose a novel UnCut finite element method (FEM) for the Poisson and Stokes equations on domains with curved boundaries represented by a level set function. Like the $φ$-FEM, the method avoids numerical integration over cut subregions of boundary elements, but introduces a novel least-squares formulation that minimizes an $H^{-1}$ residual of the governing equations. This formulation ensures stability without requiring large stabilization parameters, thereby eliminating the need for user-tuned penalty parameters and improving the robustness of the computation. Optimal-order convergence of the UnCut FEM solutions is rigorously established in the $H^1$ norm for both the Poisson and Stokes equations, and numerical experiments are presented to support the theoretical analysis.

</details>


### [25] [A posteriori error analysis for the second-order BDF method for the Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02021)
*Stefan Karch*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper derives the first rigorous a posteriori error estimates for the tangent plane scheme (TPS) discretization of the Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert (LLG) equation, using BDF(2) in time and arbitrary-degree finite elements in space, providing computable upper bounds for discretization errors on adaptive meshes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While the tangent plane scheme is a well-established discretization method for the LLG equation, no rigorous a posteriori error estimates have been established, which hinders the development of fully adaptive algorithms.

Method: The authors derive a posteriori error estimators for the TPS based on second-order backward differentiation formula (BDF(2)) for temporal discretization and finite elements of arbitrary polynomial degree for spatial discretization.

Result: The proposed estimators provide computable upper bounds for both temporal and spatial discretization errors on adaptive meshes with variable time-step sizes.

Conclusion: This work establishes the mathematical foundation necessary for fully adaptive algorithms for the LLG equation.

Abstract: The tangent plane scheme (TPS) is a well-established discretization of the Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert (LLG) equation. However, rigorous a posteriori error estimates have not been established. In this work, we derive a rigorous a posteriori error estimate for the TPS based on the second-order backward differentiation formula (BDF(2)) in time and finite elements of arbitrary polynomial degree in space. The proposed estimators provide computable upper bounds for the temporal and spatial discretization errors on adaptive meshes with variable time-step sizes. This result establishes the mathematical foundation for fully adaptive algorithms for the LLG equation.

</details>


### [26] [Finite element discretization of Yang--Mills connections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02108)
*Geir Bogfjellmo,Charles Curry,Andreas Myklebust*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A finite element method for Yang–Mills connections on nontrivial principal bundles with abelian structure group, discretizing local connection forms in broken finite element exterior calculus spaces and enforcing internal jump conditions weakly via Lagrange multipliers, with well-posedness proof, convergence estimate, and numerical validation on the Hopf fibration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Developing a numerical method for Yang–Mills connections on nontrivial principal bundles, which is challenging due to the need to handle bundle topology and transition functions in a discrete setting.

Method: Uses broken finite element exterior calculus spaces to discretize local connection forms, and enforces the internal jump conditions induced by transition functions weakly through Lagrange multipliers, leading to a saddle-point formulation.

Result: Proves well-posedness of the saddle-point problem, derives an a priori convergence estimate, and demonstrates linear convergence in a numerical experiment on the Hopf fibration with piecewise linear mesh approximation of the sphere.

Conclusion: The method provides a valid and convergent finite element approach for Yang–Mills connections on nontrivial abelian principal bundles, validated both theoretically and numerically.

Abstract: We propose a finite element method for Yang--Mills connections on nontrivial principal bundles with abelian structure group. Local connection forms satisfy internal jump conditions induced by the transition functions of the bundle. We discretize these forms in broken finite element exterior calculus spaces and enforce the jump conditions weakly by Lagrange multipliers. We prove well-posedness of the resulting saddle-point problem and derive an a priori convergence estimate. A numerical experiment for the Hopf fibration illustrates the method and exhibits linear convergence when the sphere is approximated by a piecewise linear mesh.

</details>


### [27] [Localized pointwise a posteriori error estimates for nonconforming finite element methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02132)
*Yongxing Guo,Yuwen Li*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper establishes localized pointwise a posteriori error estimates for nonconforming finite element discretizations of Poisson and biharmonic equations, covering the Crouzeix-Raviart method and Morley discretization respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain localized pointwise a posteriori error estimates for nonconforming finite element methods, which require new techniques for bounding the L1 norms of derivatives of regularized Green's functions.

Method: The authors design two novel weight functions that enable sharp estimates of the L1 norms of derivatives of a regularized Green's function for the biharmonic equation, which serve as the key ingredient for deriving localized error estimates.

Result: Localized pointwise a posteriori estimates are derived: for the Poisson problem, controlling function-value and broken gradient errors of the Crouzeix-Raviart method; for the biharmonic equation, controlling the local Hessian error of the Morley discretization.

Conclusion: The paper successfully establishes localized pointwise a posteriori error bounds for nonconforming FEMs by introducing novel weight functions that provide sharp estimates for the regularized Green's function derivatives.

Abstract: This paper establishes localized pointwise a posteriori error estimates for nonconforming finite element discretizations of the Poisson and biharmonic equations. For the Poisson problem, we derive localized estimates for the function-value and broken gradient errors of the Crouzeix--Raviart method. For the Morley discretization of the biharmonic equation, we derive a localized a posteriori estimate that controls the local Hessian error. The key ingredient is the design of two novel weight functions that facilitate sharp estimates of the \(L^1\) norms of derivatives of a regularized Green's function for the biharmonic equation.

</details>


### [28] [Discontinuous Galerkin Semidiscretization of the Information Geometric Regularized Compressible Euler Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02223)
*Brook Eyob,Jesus Arias,Spencer H. Bryngelson,Florian Schäfer*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper develops a discontinuous Galerkin (DG) discretization of the information geometric regularization (IGR) framework for compressible Euler equations. The method stabilizes shocks at the PDE level by augmenting the Euler equations with an entropic pressure from an auxiliary elliptic equation, eliminating the need for limiters or artificial viscosity while preserving fine-scale flow features.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Shock stabilization in compressible Euler flows remains a central challenge for high-order numerical methods. Existing shock-capturing approaches (limiters, artificial viscosity, reconstruction-based methods) involve tradeoffs between robustness, accuracy, preservation of fine-scale flow features, and computational complexity.

Method: A DG discretization of the IGR framework is developed. The Euler equations are augmented with an entropic pressure Σ obtained from an auxiliary elliptic equation. The regularization enters only through the augmented pressure P+Σ in the Euler fluxes, preserving the conservative structure using a common approximation space for both hyperbolic and elliptic equations.

Result: Numerical experiments in 1D and 2D show the formulation stabilizes shocks without shock-capturing limiters or artificial viscosity. The IGR-DG method resolves increasingly finer-scale flow features as polynomial order increases compared to characteristic TVB-limited DG. The entropic pressure remains localized to strong compression regions with minimal activation in smooth regions.

Conclusion: The IGR-DG method provides selective PDE-level regularization that stabilizes shocks while preserving underlying solutions elsewhere, though positivity-preserving methods may still be required when density or pressure approaches zero.

Abstract: Shock stabilization in compressible Euler flows remains a central challenge for high-order numerical methods. Existing shock-capturing approaches, including limiters, artificial viscosity, and reconstruction-based methods, involve tradeoffs between robustness, accuracy, preservation of fine-scale flow features, and computational complexity. In this work, we develop a discontinuous Galerkin (DG) discretization of the information geometric regularization (IGR) framework introduced by Cao and Schäfer for the compressible Euler equations. The method regularizes shocks at the PDE level by augmenting the Euler equations with the entropic pressure $Σ$, obtained from an auxiliary elliptic equation. Within the DG formulation, the regularization enters only through the augmented pressure $P+Σ$ in the Euler fluxes, preserving the conservative structure of the discretization while using a common approximation space for both the hyperbolic and elliptic equations. Numerical experiments spanning one and two-dimensional benchmark problems show the proposed formulation stabilizes shocks without shock-capturing limiters or artificial viscosity, although positivity-preserving methods may still be required when the density or pressure approaches zero. Compared with a characteristic TVB-limited DG formulation, the IGR-DG method resolves increasingly finer-scale flow features as the polynomial order is increased while maintaining stable shock resolution. The entropic pressure remains localized to regions of strong compression with minimal activation in smooth regions of the flow, providing selective PDE-level regularization while preserving the underlying solution elsewhere.

</details>


### [29] [A Least-Squares Weak Galerkin Method for the Biharmonic Cauchy Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02317)
*Chunmei Wang,Shangyou Zhang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper develops a least-squares weak Galerkin (LS-WG) finite element method for the Cauchy problem of the biharmonic equation by reformulating it as a coupled system of two second-order equations, yielding a symmetric positive definite system that avoids inf-sup conditions and C¹-conforming spaces while supporting general polygonal meshes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the Cauchy problem of the biharmonic equation by overcoming limitations of existing methods, namely the discrete inf-sup condition required by mixed finite element methods and the difficulty of constructing globally C¹-conforming finite element spaces.

Method: Reformulates the fourth-order biharmonic equation as a coupled system of two second-order equations, discretized using discrete weak Laplacian operators on weak finite element spaces within a least-squares formulation, yielding a symmetric positive definite linear system.

Result: The method establishes uniqueness of the discrete LS-WG solution and derives optimal-order error estimates in a discrete energy norm, with numerical experiments confirming theoretical convergence rates and demonstrating accuracy, robustness, and effectiveness.

Conclusion: The proposed LS-WG method is effective for the biharmonic Cauchy problem, offering flexibility with polygonal meshes and avoiding inf-sup conditions and C¹-conformity requirements while achieving optimal convergence.

Abstract: We develop a least-squares weak Galerkin (LS-WG) finite element method for the Cauchy problem of the biharmonic equation. The proposed approach reformulates the fourth-order equation as a coupled system of two second-order equations, which are discretized using discrete weak Laplacian operators on weak finite element spaces. The resulting least-squares formulation yields a symmetric positive definite linear system, thereby eliminating the discrete inf-sup condition required by mixed finite element methods while avoiding the construction of globally $C^1$-conforming finite element spaces. Furthermore, the weak Galerkin framework naturally accommodates general polygonal meshes, offering considerable flexibility in mesh generation and approximation. Under the assumption that the continuous biharmonic Cauchy problem admits a unique solution, we establish the uniqueness of the discrete LS-WG solution and derive optimal-order error estimates in a discrete energy norm. Numerical experiments confirm the theoretical convergence rates and demonstrate the accuracy, robustness, and effectiveness of the proposed method.

</details>


### [30] [A Unified Kullback--Leibler Divergence Analysis of Generative Diffusion Models via Entropy Production Rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02406)
*Han Wu,Zhiwen Zhang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper introduces a unified framework for error analysis of generative models via entropy production rate of forward-reverse diffusion pairs, decomposing terminal KL divergence into initialization, score approximation, and time-discretization errors. It achieves a sharp O(h²) convergence rate for Euler-Maruyama samplers and unifies score-based SDEs, probability-flow ODEs, and stochastic interpolants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing error analyses of generative models rely on Girsanov-based path-space approaches that yield suboptimal O(h) convergence rates. A unified, sharp framework is needed to decompose error sources and understand the trade-offs between deterministic and stochastic sampling.

Method: The framework analyzes entropy production at the level of marginal distributions (rather than path space) for a forward-reverse diffusion process pair. The entropy production rate admits a closed velocity form identity whose time integral decomposes the terminal KL divergence into initialization error, score approximation error, and time-discretization error. Varying diffusion coefficients within a single inequality unifies different generative model classes.

Result: Achieves a sharp O(h²) convergence rate for the Euler-Maruyama sampler, improving upon the O(h) rates from Girsanov's path-space analyses. The framework unifies analysis of score-based SDEs, probability-flow ODEs, and stochastic interpolants, and numerical experiments confirm the predicted scaling with step size and terminal time.

Conclusion: The entropy production rate framework provides a sharper, unified error analysis for generative models by working at the marginal distribution level, revealing optimal convergence rates and clarifying the trade-off between deterministic and stochastic sampling strategies.

Abstract: We introduce a unified framework for the error analysis of generative models based on the entropy production rate of the forward-reverse diffusion process pair. For a pair of continuity equation flows, the rate admits a closed velocity form identity whose time integral decomposes the terminal Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergence into the sum of an initialization error, a score approximation error, and a time-discretization error. By analyzing the entropy production at the level of marginal distributions, rather than in path space, our framework yields a sharp convergence rate of $\mathcal{O}(h^2)$
  for the Euler-Maruyama sampler, where $h$
  is the step size. This improves upon the $\mathcal{O}(h)$ rates typically obtained from Girsanov's path-space analyses. Furthermore, our framework unifies the analysis of score-based SDEs, probability-flow ODEs, and stochastic interpolants by varying diffusion coefficients within a single inequality, revealing the trade-off between deterministic and stochastic sampling. Numerical experiments confirm the predicted scaling with step size and terminal time.

</details>


### [31] [Generalised projective integration scheme in equation-free multiscale modelling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02419)
*Tanay Kumar Karmakar,Durga Charan Dalal*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper introduces a generalized projective integration (GPI) scheme for multiscale problems where spectral properties vary over time, requiring adaptive macro-, meso-, and micro-time steps. The scheme unifies existing multiscale methods, includes a comprehensive stability analysis with detailed study of stability region splitting, and develops problem-dependent strategies for selecting time steps and burst lengths. Validation across three representative problems with distinct spectral evolution types shows GPI outperforms existing projective integration methods and stiff solvers on multiple metrics including accuracy, computational time, memory usage, and number of micro time steps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fixed macro-, meso-, micro-time steps and burst lengths fail when a system's spectrum varies significantly over time, so adaptive, locally informed strategies are needed to handle time-dependent spectral variation and dynamically evolving scale separation in multiscale simulations.

Method: The paper proposes a generalized projective integration (GPI) scheme that unifies and extends existing multiscale methodologies. It includes a comprehensive stability analysis, particularly investigating the splitting of the stability region, and develops problem-dependent strategies for selecting micro-, meso-, and macro-time steps as well as burst lengths.

Result: The GPI scheme was validated on three representative problems with distinct types of spectral evolution, evaluated against existing projective integration methods and widely used stiff solvers on five criteria: number of micro time steps, accuracy, computational time, memory usage, and proportion of micro-scale simulations. The results show GPI consistently outperforms existing methods across all performance metrics.

Conclusion: The generalized projective integration scheme with adaptive step selection provides a more flexible and powerful computational paradigm for multiscale problems with time-varying spectral characteristics, consistently outperforming existing methods in terms of overall performance.

Abstract: When the spectrum of a system varies significantly over time, fixed choices of macro-, meso-, and micro-time steps, as well as burst lengths, become inadequate, necessitating adaptive and locally informed strategies. To address these challenges, this article proposes a novel and flexible generalised projective integration (GPI) scheme, designed to accommodate time-dependent spectral variation and dynamically evolving scale separation. The proposed framework unifies and extends several existing multiscale methodologies, thereby offering a more general and adaptable computational paradigm. A comprehensive stability analysis of the GPI scheme is carried out, including a detailed investigation of the splitting of the stability region, which forms a central component of this work. Furthermore, problem-dependent strategies for selecting the micro-, meso-, and macro-time steps, as well as the burst length, are developed and their impacts are systematically validated through numerical experiments. To assess the effectiveness of the proposed scheme, three representative problems with distinct types of spectral evolution are considered. The performance of the proposed GPI scheme is evaluated and compared with several existing projective integration methods as well as some widely used stiff solvers, based on (i) number of micro time steps, (ii) accuracy, (iii) computational time, (iv) memory usage and (v) proportion of micro-scale simulations. The results demonstrate that the GPI scheme consistently outperforms the existing methods in terms of performance.

</details>


### [32] [On calculating polar solvation energy of nonrigid proteins in the Poisson-Boltzmann theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02525)
*Matthias Dogbatsey,Yuanzhen Shao,Emil Alexov,Shan Zhao*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper presents a generalized Poisson-Boltzmann (PB) framework that accommodates nonrigid protein conformational changes between vacuum and water phases, eliminating self-energy artifacts in polar solvation energy calculations. It handles both regularized PB models (via analytical self-energy cancellation) and non-regularized solvers like APBS/DelPhi (via a thermodynamic cycle with Coulombic correction), validated across sharp- and diffuse-interface PB models and various numerical solvers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard PB theory assumes rigid protein structures, but proteins undergo conformational changes when transitioning from vacuum to water. This structural change affects polar solvation energy calculations, yet current PB frameworks do not properly account for nonrigid conformational changes and suffer from self-energy artifacts.

Method: The authors develop a generalized PB framework with two approaches: (1) for regularized PB models, self-energies in water and vacuum states are analytically canceled using Green's functions that capture charge singularities; (2) for non-regularized PB solvers (APBS, DelPhi), a thermodynamic cycle is proposed that adds a Coulombic correction in vacuum. The framework supports both sharp-interface and diffuse-interface PB models.

Result: The generalized PB theory was validated using a perturbed two-atom system and a diverse set of proteins with different vacuum/water structures. Results demonstrate accuracy and robustness across different interface models (sharp vs. diffuse) and numerical solvers.

Conclusion: The generalized PB framework successfully addresses nonrigid conformational changes in solvation energy calculations without self-energy artifacts, providing a broadly applicable solution independent of the choice of PB model or numerical solver.

Abstract: The Poisson-Boltzmann (PB) theory is a cornerstone of implicit solvent models for electrostatic analysis, and has found a great success in various biomolecular applications. However, in calculating polar solvation energy, one should consider that the structure of the protein changes upon transition from vacuum to water phases. To address this, here we report for the first time a generalized PB framework capable of accommodating nonrigid conformational changes without suffering from self-energy artifacts. For regularized PB models, in which the charge singularities are captured by the Green's functions, self-energies in the water and vacuum states will be analytically canceled. For non-regularized PB solvers, such as APBS and DelPhi, a simple thermodynamic cycle is proposed for nonrigid proteins by adding a Coulombic correction in vacuum. The generalized PB theory is validated using a perturbed two-atom system and a diverse set of proteins with different structures in vacuum and water, demonstrating its accuracy and robustness, regardless of the choice of sharp-interface and diffuse-interface PB models and different numerical solvers.

</details>

### [33] [On "discrete" solutions of the Euler system of gas dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00126)
*Anna Abbatiello,Eduard Feireisl*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper identifies a class of "wild" initial data for the Euler system of gas dynamics that admit infinitely many physically admissible (entropy) weak solutions via convex integration. These solutions are discrete (attaining finitely many constant states), have increasing entropy profiles, and converge to a prescribed terminal entropy state as time goes to infinity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Convex integration reveals disturbing non-uniqueness results for the Euler system of gas dynamics, showing dense sets of wild initial data with infinitely many admissible weak solutions. The authors aim to characterize these initial data more precisely by identifying conditions under which such wild behavior occurs with controlled structure.

Method: The authors employ convex integration techniques to construct weak solutions to the Euler system and identify a specific class of initial data. They construct discrete solutions that attain only finitely many constant states, whose entropy profiles increase monotonically over time and reach a prescribed terminal entropy as time tends to infinity.

Result: The paper identifies an explicit class of initial data for which there exist families of weak solutions with increasing entropy profiles, discrete structure (finitely many constant states), and convergence to a prescribed terminal entropy profile in the infinite time limit.

Conclusion: The constructed class of initial data demonstrates the non-uniqueness of entropy-admissible solutions to the Euler system in a controlled and structured manner, further highlighting the limitations of standard well-posedness concepts for gas dynamics.

Abstract: The method of Convex Integration has revealed a number of rather disturbing facts concerning well-posedness of the Euler system of gas dynamics. In particular, there is a dense set of "wild" initial data, for which the problem admits infinitely many physically admissible (entropy) weak solutions. We identify the class of initial data enjoying the following properties: (a) they give rise to a family of weak solutions with increasing entropy profiles; (b) the solutions are "discrete", meaning they attain only a finite number of constant states; (c) the solutions reach a prescribed terminal entropy profile when time goes to infinity.

</details>


### [34] [Inertial manifolds for the nonlocal parabolic problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00197)
*Xiaoqing Yang,Alexandre N. Carvalho,Chunyou Sun*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops an abstract framework for proving the existence of inertial manifolds for nonlocal parabolic problems by modifying the nonlocal term outside the absorbing ball to derive a spectral gap condition. The framework is applied to establish inertial manifold existence for a 2D nonlocal reaction-diffusion equation on a square domain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inertial manifolds are finite-dimensional invariant manifolds that capture the long-term dynamics of infinite-dimensional dynamical systems. Extending this theory to nonlocal parabolic problems is challenging because standard spectral gap conditions are difficult to satisfy directly, motivating the need for a novel abstract framework with a modified nonlocal term.

Method: The authors construct an abstract framework for inertial manifolds of nonlocal parabolic problems. They derive a spectral gap condition by suitably modifying the nonlocal term outside of the absorbing ball, which allows the required spectral properties to hold within the relevant region of phase space.

Result: The derived spectral gap condition enables the establishment of an inertial manifold for the two-dimensional nonlocal reaction-diffusion equation on a square domain, demonstrating the applicability of the abstract framework.

Conclusion: The paper successfully generalizes inertial manifold theory to a class of nonlocal parabolic problems and provides a concrete existence result for 2D nonlocal reaction-diffusion equations, broadening the scope of finite-dimensional reduction for nonlocal dynamics.

Abstract: This paper provides an abstract framework for studying inertial manifolds associated with a class of nonlocal parabolic problems. In particular, we derive a corresponding spectral gap condition by suitably modifying the nonlocal term outside of the absorbing ball. As an application, we establish the existence of an inertial manifolds for the two-dimensional nonlocal reaction-diffusion equation on a square domain.

</details>


### [35] [Regularity results for elliptic equations on cones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00199)
*Carlo Alberto Antonini,Filomena Pacella,Camilla Chiara Polvara,Luigi Provenzano*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes global regularity results for elliptic problems (Poisson and p-Laplacian equations) in spherical sectors, showing gradient boundedness when the first eigenvalue of the Laplace-Beltrami operator satisfies λ₁(D) ≥ N-1, with the condition proven sharp via Maz'ya's example, and further establishing weighted Lipschitz regularity and second-order regularity for general spherical sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and characterize when global gradient estimates and regularity hold for elliptic boundary value problems in domains with conical singularities (spherical sectors), which is critical for PDE theory on non-smooth domains.

Method: The paper analyzes Dirichlet and Neumann elliptic problems in spherical sectors by relating gradient boundedness to the spectral condition on the first nontrivial eigenvalue λ₁(D) of the Laplace-Beltrami operator on the cross-section domain D, and extends results to p-Laplacian equations via weighted Lipschitz estimates and second-order regularity analysis.

Result: Gradient boundedness for Poisson equations holds when λ₁(D) ≥ N-1, and this condition is sharp (demonstrated by Maz'ya's counterexample). For p-Laplacian equations in general spherical sectors, weighted global Lipschitzianity and second-order regularity are established.

Conclusion: The spectral condition λ₁(D) ≥ N-1 serves as a sharp threshold for gradient regularity in spherical sectors, and the results extend to nonlinear p-Laplacian problems with weighted Lipschitz and second-order regularity guarantees.

Abstract: We study global regularity of solutions to Dirichlet or Neumann elliptic problems in spherical sectors $S_{D,R}$ of radius $R>0$ in $\mathbb{R}^N, N\ge 2$, where $D$ is the bounded domain on the unit sphere $\mathbb{S}^{N-1}$ which spans the spherical sector. One of the main results shows that boundedness of the gradient of the solutions of Poisson equations holds whenever $λ_1(D)\ge N-1$, where $λ_1(D)$ is the first nontrivial eigenvalue of the Laplace Beltrami operator $-Δ_{\mathbb{S}^{N-1}}$ on the domain $D$ with Dirichlet or Neumann boundary conditions on $\partial D$.
  As an example of Maz'ya shows, the condition on the eigenvalue is sharp.
  For general spherical sectors and for $p$-Laplacian equations, $p>1$ we prove weighted global lipschitzianity of the solutions, as well as second order regularity.

</details>


### [36] [Delayed Dissipation for Two-Dimensional Vortex Sheets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00234)
*Victor Armegioiu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper quantifies viscous energy loss for two-dimensional Delort vortex sheets, improving the known bound from O(|log ν|^{-1/2}) to O(1/|log ν|), and disproves a conjecture by De Rosa and Marcotullio. The result extends to growing observation times, giving polynomial lower bounds on the energetic lifetime of the inviscid vortex-sheet model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to precisely quantify how much energy is dissipated by viscosity in two-dimensional vortex sheets (in the vanishing viscosity limit), improving previously known estimates and resolving an open conjecture. Understanding the vanishing viscosity limit for vortex sheets is important for modeling fluid flows with concentrated vorticity, and the energy dissipation rate determines how long the inviscid approximation remains physically valid.

Method: The proof uses a sharp L²–H¹–H⁻¹ interpolation inequality for nonnegative densities. Unlike previous approaches that only retained the largest local mass of positive vorticity, this inequality preserves the total interaction energy of the positive vorticity component. The vorticity is decomposed as ω₀^ν = μ₀^ν + f₀^ν, where μ₀^ν is nonnegative and f₀^ν is bounded in L^p (p>1), allowing refined estimates of the dissipation integral ν∫‖ω^ν(t)‖₂²dt.

Result: The main result establishes ν∫_δ^T‖ω^ν(t)‖₂²dt ≲_{δ,T} 1/|log ν|, improving the previous O(|log ν|^{-1/2}) bound and disproving Conjecture 1.6 of De Rosa and Marcotullio. The bound remains effective for growing observation times T_ν with log T_ν = o(|log ν|), giving polynomial lower bounds (ν^{-a}) on the energetic lifetime. Under weaker L(log L)^α integrability, the rate becomes O(|log ν|^{-q_α}) with q_α = min{2α,1}. Exact radial solutions on ℝ² attain these exponents, and endpoint cases are sharp for bounded-energy L^p families.

Conclusion: The paper provides sharp energy dissipation rates for 2D Delort vortex sheets in the vanishing viscosity limit, improving previous bounds by a factor of log^{1/2} and disproving an existing conjecture. The results also significantly extend the validity window for observation times, establishing polynomial lower bounds on the energetic lifetime—a major improvement over previous sub-exponential bounds. Sharpness is demonstrated through explicit radial solutions and endpoint constructions.

Abstract: We quantify viscous energy loss for two-dimensional Delort vortex sheets. Let $u^ν$ be Leray-Hopf solutions on $\mathbb{T}^2$ with uniformly bounded kinetic energy and total vorticity variation, and write $ω_0^ν=μ_0^ν+f_0^ν$, where $μ_0^ν\geq0$ and $f_0^ν$ is bounded in $L^p$, $p>1$. For every fixed $0<δ<T$, $ν\int_δ^T\|ω^ν(t)\|_2^2\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim_{δ,T}\frac{1}{|\logν|}$. This improves the $O(|\logν|^{-1/2})$ bound of De Rosa and Marcotullio under the same assumptions and thereby disproves their Conjecture 1.6 (arXiv:2602.15670, v1). The proof uses a sharp $L^2$-$H^1$-$H^{-1}$ interpolation inequality for nonnegative densities, retaining the total interaction energy of the positive vorticity rather than only its largest local mass.
  The bound also remains effective when the observation time grows with the Reynolds number. If the initial velocities are relatively compact in $L^2$, the loss still vanishes whenever $\log T_ν=o(|\logν|)$; in particular, any prescribed energy loss must wait at least until $ν^{-a}$ for some $a>0$. Previous estimates covered only $T_ν=o(\exp(|\logν|^κ))$, $κ<1/2$, so this gives a polynomial lower bound on the energetic lifetime of the inviscid vortex-sheet model. If instead $f_0^ν$ is bounded in $L(\log L)^α$, the rate is $O(|\logν|^{-q_α})$, $q_α=\min\{2α,1\}$, and the loss vanishes when $\log T_ν=o(|\logν|^{q_α})$. On $\mathbb{R}^2$, exact radial solutions attain these exponents for $0<α\leq1/2$. At the endpoint, a bounded-energy $L^p$ family attains the rate $1/|\logν|$, while every fixed radial datum dissipates $o(1/|\logν|)$ and can lose a fixed amount of energy only on the diffusive scale $1/ν$.

</details>


### [37] [The good commutator approach to global asymptotics for the Schrödinger equation with variable coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00268)
*Sung-Jin Oh,Federico Pasqualotto,Ning Tang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a robust physical-space method for establishing time decay and global asymptotics of variable-coefficient Schrödinger equations in (3+1) dimensions, with applications to quasilinear Schrödinger equations with cubic Hamiltonian nonlinearities, including cases with obstacles and trapped bicharacteristics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend physical-space methods (commuting vector fields, testing-by-wave-packets) to variable-coefficient Schrödinger equations, where classical Klainerman methods do not directly apply, enabling global existence and asymptotics for quasilinear problems with obstacles and trapped trajectories.

Method: Three main ingredients: (1) a "good commutator" extending Klainerman's commuting vector field method (building on Cuccagna-Georgiev-Visciglia, Rodnianski-Tao, Ifrim-Tataru, Ifrim-Koch-Tataru); (2) Ifrim-Tataru's testing-by-wave-packets method for global asymptotics and sharp coefficient decay assumptions; (3) a novel "two-scale elliptic analysis" technique for systematic analysis of the good commutator's control.

Result: New small data global existence and asymptotics results for quasilinear Schrödinger equations with cubic Hamiltonian nonlinearity and variable coefficients, valid even outside obstacles and in the presence of suitably unstable trapped bicharacteristics.

Conclusion: The combined techniques significantly broaden the applicability of physical-space methods to a wider class of variable-coefficient nonlinear problems.

Abstract: We present a robust physical-space approach to establish time decay and global asymptotics of solutions to variable-coefficient Schrödinger equations in (3+1)-dimensions. As an immediate nonlinear application, we obtain new small data global existence and asymptotics results for quasilinear Schrödinger equations with cubic, Hamiltonian nonlinearity, variable coefficients in their linear part, and possibly outside obstacles, even in the presence of trapped bicharacteristics (provided that they are suitably unstable).
  Our approach relies on three primary ingredients. First is the concept of a good commutator, which extends Klainerman's classical commuting vector field method, and develops upon earlier approaches of Cuccagna-Georgiev-Visciglia and Rodnianski-Tao in the variable-coefficient case, and of Ifrim-Tataru, Ifrim-Koch-Tataru in the nonlinear case. Second, we apply Ifrim and Tataru's testing-by-wave-packets method, which is key for obtaining global asymptotics and handling sharp decay assumptions on the coefficients. Finally, we introduce a systematic technique for analyzing the control provided by the good commutator, termed two-scale elliptic analysis. Together, these techniques significantly broaden the applicability of physical-space methods to a wider class of variable-coefficient nonlinear problems.

</details>


### [38] [A Phase-Field Method for Curvature Flow of Networks with Triple Junction Drag](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00293)
*Yuchuan Yang,Selim Esedoglu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a new phase-field (diffuse interface) approximation for multiphase curvature motion with triple junction drag, derived from a variational perspective (minimizing movements), with convergence verified via matched asymptotic expansions and numerical studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to provide a diffuse interface approach to model the sharp interface problem of multiphase curvature motion with triple junction drag, which is important for understanding microstructure evolution in polycrystalline materials during heat treatment.

Method: The method derives a diffuse interface approximation (a variant of the Allen-Cahn system) from a variational standpoint using minimizing movements with discrete-in-time convex optimization. A new integral expression counts junctions using the order parameter, and convergence to the sharp interface limit is verified via matched asymptotic expansions and numerical benchmarks.

Result: Convergence to the sharp interface limit is confirmed through both matched asymptotic expansions and numerical convergence studies against exact solutions and front-tracking benchmark solutions. The method automatically handles topological changes in the interface network.

Conclusion: The proposed phase-field method successfully approximates multiphase curvature motion with triple junction drag while retaining the key advantage of diffuse interface methods: automatic handling of topological changes in the interfacial network.

Abstract: We develop a new phase-field (diffuse interface) approximation of multiphase curvature motion with triple junction drag - an important sharp interface model for the evolution of microstructure in polycrystalline materials during heat treatment. This sharp interface model arises as gradient flow for the total length of the interfacial network with respect to a certain metric. Accordingly, we derive our diffuse interface approximation - a coupled system of partial differential equations that is a variant of the Allen-Cahn system - from a variational perspective, in the style of minimizing movements: starting from a discrete in time approximation that entails a convex optimization problem to advance from one time step to the next. In the process, we propose a simple integral expression that counts the number of junctions using the order parameter that appears to be new even for the standard multiphase Allen-Cahn system. The convergence of the resulting flow to the desired sharp interface limit is then verified via the method of matched asymptotic expansions. Numerical convergence studies against both known exact solutions as well as highly accurate benchmark solutions obtained via front tracking provide clear further evidence for this convergence. Moreover, the method retains the most desirable feature of diffuse interface methods: Automatic handling of topological changes in the network of interfaces.

</details>


### [39] [On the Robustness of Propagating Bound States in the Continuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00411)
*Lijun Yuan,Ya Yan Lu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper presents a rigorous mathematical theory for the robustness of bound states in the continuum (BICs) in 2D dielectric structures with a single periodic direction, proving convergence of perturbation series for BICs in slightly perturbed systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A fundamental question is whether BICs are robust under structural perturbations. Earlier work formally constructed BICs in perturbed systems using perturbation methods, but lacked rigorous mathematical justification. This paper aims to provide a rigorous theory for the robustness of BICs in 2D dielectric structures with a single periodic direction.

Method: The paper analyzes the solvability and provides estimates for each order in the perturbation series, then proves the convergence of the series for BICs in 2D dielectric structures with a single periodic direction.

Result: The paper establishes a rigorous theory on the robustness of BICs by proving convergence of the perturbation series for perturbed wave systems.

Conclusion: BICs can continue their existence under slight structural perturbations, and this robustness is now rigorously established through convergence proofs of the perturbation series.

Abstract: Bound states in the continuum (BICs) are localized eigenmodes with their frequencies in the radiation continuum of scattering states. The existence of a BIC implies the loss of uniqueness for scattering problems with given incident waves. Perturbed wave systems close to the ideal ones with a BIC exhibit strong resonance effects that are essential to numerous practical applications. A question of fundamental importance is whether a BIC is robust, i.e., whether it can continue its existence when the structure is slightly perturbed. In an earlier work [Yuan and Lu, Optics Letters, Vol.~42, pp.~4490-4493, 2017], for a class of BICs governed by the two-dimensional (2D) Helmholtz equation, which are not trivially protected by symmetry, we uncovered the conditions that ensure robustness and formally constructed the BIC in perturbed systems using a perturbation method. In this paper, we present a rigorous theory on the robustness of BICs in 2D dielectric structures with a single periodic direction. Specifically, we analyze the solvability and provide estimates for each order in the perturbation series, and prove the convergence of the series.

</details>

These are assembled into a contraction argument to produce the self-similar solutions.

Result: The authors construct a one-parameter family of small self-similar solutions emanating from conical initial data η_ε(0,x)=ε|x|, and identify the leading quadratic correction to the linear capillary regularization of the cone profile. The solutions demonstrate that the conical singularity is instantaneously rounded for positive time.

Conclusion: The conical singularity is instantaneously smoothed by surface tension in the 3D one-phase Muskat problem, and the self-similar structure with scaling t^{1/3} provides an explicit description of this regularization mechanism.

Abstract: We construct a one-parameter family of small self-similar solutions to the three-dimensional one-phase Muskat problem with surface tension. The solutions have the form $η_\varepsilon(t,x) = t^{1/3}U_\varepsilon(t^{-1/3}x)$ and emanate from the conical initial data $η_\varepsilon(0,x)=\varepsilon|x|$. The profiles are perturbations of the linear capillary regularization of the cone, and we identify the leading quadratic correction. The proof combines a raywise inverse estimate for the linear similarity operator, a favorable high--high-to-low cancellation in the quadratic term, and finite-order tame estimates for the Dirichlet--Neumann operator on asymptotically conical graphs. These estimates yield the solutions by a contraction argument and show that the conical singularity is instantaneously rounded for positive time.

Abstract: We study the periodic homogenization of a class of viscous Hamilton--Jacobi equations with fast dependence on the unknown. This problem combines features of first-order Hamilton--Jacobi equations with \(u^ε/ε\)-periodic Hamiltonians and semilinear heat equations with rapidly oscillating positive potentials. In this paper, we prove qualitative homogenization results for $H(y,s,p)=F(s,p)+ηW(y,s,p)$ when $|η|$ is sufficiently small, with the smallness threshold depending on the Lipschitz constant of the initial datum, and establish a large-time averaging result for a general class of evolutionary cell problems.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem for the three-dimensional barotropic compressible Navier--Stokes equations with a time-independent potential force near a spatially nonconstant stationary state. The potential is controlled in unweighted homogeneous Besov spaces; in particular, no polynomial spatial-weight condition involving $(1+|x|)^j\nabla^jφ$ is imposed. For initial data relative to the stationary state that are sufficiently small in $\dot H^{\frac12-δ}\cap\dot H^3$, we establish the existence and uniqueness of a global strong solution in $H^3$, while allowing the initial $L^2$ norm to be arbitrarily large. If the initial data are bounded in $\dot B^s_{2,\infty}$ for $s\in[-\frac32,-1)$, then the solution and its first spatial derivative decay at the optimal rates $(1+t)^{-\frac{k-s}{2}}$ with $k=0$ and $1$, respectively. The analysis relies on refined homogeneous energy estimates and a frequency-localized description for the dissipative and asymptotic structures of the system.

The input-output operator is shown to be compact and Lipschitz continuous, confirming the ill-posed nature of the inverse problem. Abstract: We investigate a coupled thermoelastic plate system consisting of a fourth-order displacement equation and a heat evolution equation linked through a spatially varying coupling factor $α(x)$. The model accounts for thermoelastic interactions through the operators $\operatorname{div}(α(x)\nabla θ)$ and $\operatorname{div}(α(x)\nabla u_t)$. We establish the well-posedness of the direct problem under homogeneous Neumann conditions for $u$ and Dirichlet conditions for $θ$, deriving optimal energy estimates and demonstrating continuous dependence of solutions on the given data. We further introduce an input-output operator corresponding to the considered inverse problem and show that it is compact and Lipschitz continuous, confirming the ill-posed nature of the associated inverse problem. Using these properties, the inverse problem is formulated as a minimization problem for the Tikhonov functional, and we establish the existence of a minimizer.

### [44] [Spinorial Div-Curl Structure and Bilinear Null-Form Estimates for Dirac Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00477)
*Jiong-Yue Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper identifies a physical-space balance-law mechanism underlying bilinear null forms for the free Dirac equation in 3D. By decomposing spinors into eigenspaces of the directional Dirac symbol, the authors derive one-dimensional balance laws and a div-curl interaction estimate that controls mixed products of charge densities. Combined with angular localization, this yields frequency-localized L2 spacetime estimates preserving the first-order formulation, with gains over direct product bounds and channel-dependent refinements in the massive case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to uncover the physical mechanism behind bilinear null forms for the Dirac equation, seeking a deeper structural understanding beyond purely algebraic cancellation. They want to relate the algebraic null-form condition to actual balance laws in physical space, and obtain improved spacetime estimates that preserve the natural first-order structure of the Dirac equation.

Method: For each spatial direction, spinors are decomposed into two eigenspaces of the directional Dirac symbol, where principal parts propagate in opposite directions while transverse derivatives and the mass term couple them. After transverse integration, charge densities satisfy 1D balance laws, and a div-curl interaction estimate controls their mixed product. The algebraic anticommutation condition defining the spinorial null form is shown to exchange exactly these two eigenspaces, connecting the algebraic cancellation to the balance-law-selected interaction. Angular localization is combined with this to obtain frequency-localized L2 spacetime estimates.

Result: The method yields frequency-localized L2 spacetime estimates preserving the first-order Dirac formulation. In the massless case, the estimate has the same lower-frequency scaling as the 3D wave null-form estimate and gains half a derivative over the direct product bound. In the massive case, a channel-dependent refinement is obtained: for pseudoscalar interactions, anticommutation with the full massive Dirac Hamiltonian gives an additional frequency-to-mass factor K/m for low-frequency interactions within the same energy branch, while this gain is absent in the scalar channel. The bilinear estimates also yield factorized bounds for cubic spinorial null forms.

Conclusion: The paper establishes that bilinear null forms for the Dirac equation have a physical-space interpretation in terms of balance laws governing oppositely-propagating modes, providing both sharpened estimates (half-derivative gain, massive refinements) and a structural framework applicable to cubic interactions in nonlinear Dirac models.

Abstract: We identify a physical-space balance-law mechanism underlying bilinear null forms for the free Dirac equation in three space dimensions. For each spatial direction, we decompose a spinor into the two eigenspaces of the directional Dirac symbol. The principal parts of the corresponding modes propagate in opposite directions, while the transverse derivatives and the mass term couple them. After integration over the transverse variables, their charge densities satisfy a pair of one-dimensional balance laws, and a div--curl interaction estimate controls the mixed product of these densities. The algebraic anticommutation condition defining the spinorial null form exchanges exactly the same two eigenspaces. This identifies the algebraic cancellation with the interaction selected by the balance laws. Combined with angular localization, the argument yields frequency-localized $L^2$ spacetime estimates while preserving the natural first-order formulation of the Dirac equation. In the massless case, the estimate has the same lower-frequency scaling as the three-dimensional wave null-form estimate and gains half a derivative over the direct product bound. In the massive case, we obtain a channel-dependent refinement. For the pseudoscalar interaction, anticommutation with the full massive Dirac Hamiltonian gives an additional frequency-to-mass factor $K/m$ for low-frequency interactions within the same energy branch, where $K$ is the frequency scale and $m$ is the mass. This gain is absent in the scalar channel. The bilinear estimates also yield factorized bounds for cubic spinorial null forms arising in nonlinear Dirac models.

</details>

Conclusion: The work establishes a new family of nonradial stationary solutions for the SQG equation, demonstrating a rich structure of steady states beyond the radial ones, and links them to the half-Yamabe equation through a geometric concentration mechanism.

Abstract: We prove the existence of infinitely many smooth nonradial stationary solutions to the surface quasi-geostrophic (SQG) equation with finite kinetic energy. Our construction is based on a family of nonradial sign-changing solutions to the two-dimensional half-Yamabe equation, obtained via a Lyapunov--Schmidt reduction and concentrated at the vertices of a regular polygon. As the number of vertices tends to infinity, the associated stationary SQG solutions converge to a radial stationary profile centered at the origin, together with a lower-order vortex sheet correction.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: The proof uses domain truncation and compactness arguments. The key technical challenge is demonstrating that the density ρ remains bounded away from zero and infinity on any compact subset of ℝ, which enables passage to the limit p→∞.

Result: The solutions converge in the singular limit p→∞ to functions (ρ,u) satisfying |∂xu|≤1, τ=π∂xu with π≥0, and the complementarity condition π(1-|∂xu|)=0 almost everywhere on ℝ. Existence of weak solutions to the limiting equation is rigorously justified.

Conclusion: The paper extends the known result from periodic domains to the whole real line, requiring novel compactness and density uniform-boundedness arguments to handle the non-compact setting.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the analysis of a one-dimensional power-law model for compressible fluid dynamics on $\mathbb{R}$, in which the shear stress takes the form $μ|\partial_{x}u|^{p-2}\partial_{x}u$, where $μ$ is the viscosity coefficient and $u$ is the velocity. We prove that, in the singular limit $p\rightarrow\infty$, the solutions converge to functions $(ρ,u)$ satisfying $|\partial_{x}u|\leq 1$, $τ= π\partial_{x}u$, $π\geq 0$, and $π(1 - |\partial_{x}u|) = 0$ a.e. on $\mathbb{R}$. Moreover, we rigorously justify the existence of weak solutions to the limiting equation. The convergence as $p \to \infty$ is obtained via domain truncation and compactness arguments, of which the key challenge is to show that the density remains bounded away from zero and infinity on any compact subset. This extends the recent result of Bresch, Burtea, and Szlenk [Nonlinearity 26 (2026), no. 5, Paper No. 055010.] from one-dimensional periodic domain to the whole real line.

</details>


### [47] [Low Mach number limit for the Navier--Stokes--Korteweg equations with a stationary force](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00727)
*Jinkai Ni,Luqi Wang,Yichi Zhang,Zhipeng Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the low Mach number limit of the 3D compressible Navier-Stokes-Korteweg equations under a small stationary external force, proving global strong solution existence for ill-prepared perturbations and deriving convergence rates in mixed Besov and Lebesgue norms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the presence of the Korteweg (capillary) term affects the incompressible limit of the compressible Navier-Stokes equations, particularly since the Korteweg term alters both the elliptic structure of stationary solutions and the dispersive mechanism of acoustic waves.

Method: The authors construct small stationary solutions uniform in the Mach number, then prove global well-posedness for ill-prepared perturbations using high-order energy estimates, low-frequency Besov estimates, and a Kawashima-type compensating functional. They work in Korteweg-symmetric variables where spectral projections are uniformly bounded Fourier multipliers, and decompose Duhamel sources according to time-integrability and frequency behavior, combining dyadic dispersive estimates, high-frequency damping, and maximal regularity for the heat equation.

Result: They establish the existence of unique global strong solutions and obtain the convergence rate $\epsilon^{\min\{1/r,\,1/2-1/p\}}$ in mixed Besov norms $L^r(0,\infty;\dot B^s_{p,1})$, which via Besov embeddings yields quantitative convergence in mixed Lebesgue norms $L^r(0,\infty;L^p)$.

Conclusion: The low Mach number limit is rigorously justified for the Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system in 3D with quantitative convergence rates, showing that the Korteweg term's modification of acoustic dispersion (wave-like at low frequencies, Schrödinger-like at high frequencies) can be handled through a tailored analytic framework.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the low Mach number limit for the three-dimensional compressible Navier--Stokes--Korteweg equations in the whole space under a small stationary external force. We first construct a family of small stationary solutions uniformly with respect to the Mach number $\eps$ and prove that both the stationary density fluctuation and the compressible component of the stationary velocity are of order $\eps^2$. For ill-prepared nonstationary perturbations around these stationary solutions, we establish the existence of unique global strong solutions by combining uniform high-order energy estimates with a low-frequency Besov estimate and a Kawashima-type compensating functional. The main difficulty is that the Korteweg term not only changes the elliptic structure of the stationary problem but also modifies the dispersive mechanism of the acoustic modes. In Korteweg-symmetric variables, the associated spectral projections are uniformly bounded zero-order Fourier multipliers, while the acoustic--capillary phase is wave-like at low frequencies and Schrödinger-like at high frequencies. Since the source terms generated by the stationary coefficients are not generally integrable in time, we decompose the Duhamel source according to its time-integrability and frequency behavior. Dyadic dispersive estimates, high-frequency damping estimates, and maximal regularity for the heat equation yield the global-in-time convergence rate $\eps^{\min\{1/r,\,1/2-1/p\}}$ in the mixed Besov norms \(L^r(0,\infty;\dot B^s_{p,1})\). As a consequence, Besov embeddings also yield quantitative convergence in the mixed Lebesgue norms \(L^r(0,\infty;L^p)\).

</details>


### [48] [Functional Ordinary Differential Equations and the Design of Dichromatic Lenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00753)

They then formulate the two-frequency lens design problem as a system of functional ODEs whose solvability characterizes lens existence. Using the new theorem, they prove existence of such a lens in 2D and extend the result to 3D.

Conclusion: The paper provides both a theoretical contribution to functional ODE theory and a practical application demonstrating the existence of two-frequency collimating lenses in both 2D and 3D settings.

Abstract: This paper establishes an existence and uniqueness theorem for a class of functional ordinary differential equations under assumptions weaker than those found in the literature. We then consider the problem of designing a lens that refracts rays of two distinct frequencies emitted from a point source into a common collimated beam. We derive a system of functional ordinary differential equations whose solvability characterizes the existence of such lenses. Using the existence theorem, we prove that such a lens exists in two dimensions. We then extend the corresponding result to three dimensions.

### [49] [Counterexamples to the Landis conjecture in dimensions three and higher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00802)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Landis conjecture proposes that solutions to the stationary Schrödinger equation with bounded potential cannot decay faster than exp(-c|x|) unless they are identically zero. The authors aim to test whether this conjecture holds in higher dimensions, and they find that it fails in dimensions three and above.

Method: The authors explicitly construct a nonzero function in dimensions n ≥ 3 that satisfies the stationary Schrödinger equation with a bounded, real-valued potential, and exhibits super-exponential decay of the form exp(-c|x|^(4/3)).

Result: They successfully construct such a function in every dimension three and higher, producing a solution that decays like exp(-c|x|^(4/3)), which is a counterexample to the Landis conjecture in these dimensions.

Conclusion: The Landis conjecture is false in dimensions three and higher, as demonstrated by explicit counterexamples with decay rate exp(-c|x|^(4/3)).

Abstract: In every dimension three and higher we construct a nonzero function that satisfies the stationary Schrödinger equation with bounded, real-valued potential and decays like $\exp(-c|x|^{4/3})$. This gives a counterexample to the Landis conjecture in dimension three and higher.

</details>


### [50] [The Hardy averaging operator between Orlicz spaces and a new gauge functional characterizing a given Orlicz space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00853)
*Amiran Gogatishvili,Ron Kerman,Susanna Spektor*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the optimal Orlicz domain for the Hardy averaging operator coincides with the optimal rearrangement-invariant domain, and shows every Luxemburg-Orlicz norm is equivalent to a more computable sublinear functional. <details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine optimal Orlicz domains for classical operators and make dual norm computations in interpolation theory more tractable.

Method: Proves an equivalence between Luxemburg-Orlicz norms and a simpler sublinear functional, and shows the optimal Orlicz domain for the Hardy averaging operator coincides with the optimal rearrangement-invariant domain.

Result: Optimal Orlicz-space mapping properties are obtained for the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, approximate identities, and Calderón-Zygmund singular integral operators, with optimality established for the first two.

Conclusion: The optimal Orlicz domain for the Hardy averaging operator is automatically the optimal r.i. domain, and the simplified norm formulation enables optimal mapping results for several important operators.

Abstract: We prove that the largest Orlicz space into which the Hardy averaging operator maps coincides with the largest rearrangement-invariant (r.i.) space mapping into that space; in other words, the optimal Orlicz domain is automatically the optimal r.i.\ domain. More generally, we show that every Luxemburg--Orlicz norm is equivalent to a sublinear functional which makes more computable a certain expression for a dual norm arising in the $\K$-theory of interpolation. As applications we obtain Orlicz-space mapping properties for the Hardy--Littlewood maximal function, approximate identities and Calderón--Zygmund singular integral operators. These mapping properties are shown to be optimal for the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function and the approximate identities.

</details>


### [51] [On weighted rigidity estimates for bulk and thin domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00863)
*Davit Harutyunyan,Andre. M. Rodrigues*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes weighted Geometric Rigidity Estimates and Korn's first inequalities in bulk and thin domains, where weights are nonnegative powers of the distance to part of the domain boundary. For thin domains, the distance is taken from the thin face, and constants are shown to have optimal scaling as thickness approaches zero when the mid-surface contains a flat region. <details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend existing weighted rigidity and Korn-type inequalities to settings where weights depend on the distance to only part of the boundary, and to establish sharp (optimal) scaling of constants in thin domains as thickness tends to zero, which is relevant for dimension reduction and thin structure analysis.

Method: The authors employ covering techniques originally developed by Acosta, Cejas, and Duran for weighted Poincaré inequalities, and later extended by Conti and Zwicknagl for weighted Poincaré and classical Geometric Rigidity inequalities in Lipschitz domains. However, the partial-boundary distance introduces additional challenges in the covering arguments, especially in the thin domain setting, requiring more delicate analysis.

Result: Weighted Geometric Rigidity Estimates and Korn's first inequalities are proven for bulk and thin domains with weights being powers of the distance to part of the boundary. For thin domains, the constants depend on thickness, and optimal scaling is proven when the mid-surface contains a flat region.

Conclusion: The paper successfully establishes weighted rigidity and Korn inequalities with partial-boundary distance weights, achieving optimal constant scaling in the thin domain limit. The analysis highlights that the covering arguments become substantially more delicate when the distance is measured only to part of the boundary rather than the entire boundary.

Abstract: This work is concerned with weighted Geometric Rigidity Estimates and Korn's first inequalities in bulk and thin domains. We consider weights, that are a nonnegative power of the distance function to part of the boundary of the domain. For the case of thin domains, the distance is taken to be from the thin face of the domain boundary, and the constants depend on the domain thickness and when the mid-surface contains a flat region, the constants are proven to have optimal scaling as the thickness approaches zero. We employed some covering techniques utilized by Acosta, Cejas, and Duran in [\ref{bib:Aco.Cej.Dur.}] to prove a weighted Poincaré inequality, and later by Conti and Zwicknagl in [\ref{bib:Con.Zwi.}] to prove weighted Poincaré and classical Geometric Rigidity inequalities in Lipschitz domains. However, because the distance is taken to be only from part of the boundary, the covering parts become more delicate, especially for thin domains and the analysis becomes non-straightforward.

</details>


### [52] [Free boundary space-like graphs with prescribed mean curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00887)
*Lorenzo Maniscalco*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence, uniqueness, and regularity of weak solutions to the prescribed Lorentzian mean curvature problem with capillary boundary conditions over convex bounded domains, showing the solution is W^{2,2}-regular with gradient bounded away from 1, and is the unique maximizer of an associated functional.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors address the prescribed Lorentzian mean curvature problem with homogeneous capillary boundary conditions, a problem arising in geometric analysis and relativity. The key challenge is establishing regularity and uniqueness of weak solutions, particularly excluding "light segments" where |Du|=1, which are problematic for weak solution theory.

Method: The approach involves constructing an associated functional and proving its maximizer is a weak solution. A crucial step is demonstrating that the maximizer has no light segments (segments where |Du|=1), which holds for arbitrary bounded capillary boundary data. This no-light-segment result is proven independently and is of standalone interest.

Result: The problem has a unique W^{2,2}-regular weak solution u with zero mean, satisfying |Du| ≤ 1 - θ for some θ ∈ (0,1) depending only on the data. This solution is also the unique maximizer of the associated functional.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a complete well-posedness result for the prescribed Lorentzian mean curvature problem with capillary boundary conditions, including strict gradient bounds and a variational characterization. The no-light-segments result is a general and potentially widely applicable finding.

Abstract: We address the prescribed Lorentzian mean curvature problem over a convex bounded domain $Ω$ of $\mathbb R^m$ with bounded right-hand side and homogeneous capillary boundary condition. We prove that the problem has a unique $W^{2,2}$-regular weak solution $u$ with zero mean and that $|Du| \leq 1 - θ$ for some $θ\in(0,1)$ only depending on the data. Such $u$ is also the unique maximizer of an associated functional. A key step in proving that the maximizer is a weak solution consists in showing that it has no light segments, i.e. segments along which $|Du| = 1$. This holds for arbitrary bounded capillary boundary data and can thus be an interesting result on its own.

</details>


### [53] [Observability of wave and plate equations with rough coefficients and interfaces: a multiplier approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00912)
*Sylvain Ervedoza,Alex Imba,Alberto Mercado,Axel Osses*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper derives observability properties for wave and plate equations with rough (singular) coefficients in the principal part, including interface cases, by using a regularization/approximation process. Observability inequalities are passed to the limit from smooth approximating systems to the singular limit system, yielding new observability results for interfaces meeting boundaries or multiple intersecting interfaces, under sign-constraint conditions on coefficient jumps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
