Motivation: Observability properties for wave and plate equations with rough or singular coefficients (e.g., at interfaces) are challenging to establish directly due to the lack of regularity of solutions near singular sets. The authors seek a general framework that bypasses detailed regularity analysis by leveraging uniform observability of smooth approximating systems.

Method: The approach is a regularization/limit-passing strategy: approximate a singular coefficient by a sequence of smooth coefficients such that each approximating system is uniformly observable. Then show that the observability inequalities can be passed to the limit, yielding observability for the limit (singular-coefficient) system. This is applied to cases with interfaces meeting the boundary or multiple intersecting interfaces, subject to a multiplier-type condition imposing sign constraints on coefficient jumps.

Result: New observability results are established for wave and plate equations with rough coefficients, particularly in the presence of interfaces meeting the boundary or multiple interfaces intersecting at a point, under suitable sign constraints on coefficient jumps at interfaces.

Conclusion: The regularization approach provides a powerful and general tool for deriving observability for singular-coefficient systems, avoiding detailed solution regularity analysis near singular sets, and yields new results for challenging interface configurations.

Abstract: The goal of this article is to derive observability properties for wave and plate equations with rough coefficients in the principal part, including the case of interfaces, through a regularization process. Specifically, we demonstrate that if a singular coefficient can be approximated by a sequence of smooth coefficients corresponding to uniformly observable systems, then the observability inequalities can be passed to the limit. This allows us to derive observability properties for the limit system. While this strategy is natural, we show that it can be used to establish new observability results in several settings, particularly in the presence of interfaces meeting the boundary, or multiple interfaces intersecting at a point, under a suitable multiplier-type condition that imposes sign constraints on the jumps of the coefficients at the interfaces. A key advantage of this approach is that it avoids the need for a detailed analysis of the regularity of solutions near the sets where the coefficients are singular.

</details>


### [54] [Global well-posedness and scattering for the defocusing energy supercritical NLS in high dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00951)
*Xuan Liu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper improves the conditional global existence and scattering result for the defocusing energy-supercritical nonlinear Schrödinger equation. Previous work by Killip-Visan and Li-Li required restrictive conditions on the parameter p for dimensions d≥8, but this paper relaxes the upper bound on the critical Sobolev exponent sc from sc < (d+2−√((d−2)²−16))/4 to sc < 1+p, thereby covering all cases in the local existence range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the range of validity for the global existence and scattering theorem of the energy-supercritical NLS equation, which is an important open problem in dispersive PDEs. The previous results in high dimensions (d≥8) had restrictive conditions on the nonlinearity exponent p.

Method: The paper establishes new nonlinear estimates that allow the improvement of the upper bound on the critical Sobolev exponent sc from the previous bound to sc < 1+p.

Result: The new bound sc < 1+p covers all cases where p lies in the local existence range, significantly improving the applicability of the global existence and scattering result.

Conclusion: The authors successfully remove previously restrictive technical conditions on the nonlinearity, making the conditional scattering theorem valid across the full local existence range for dimensions d≥5.

Abstract: We consider the defocusing energy-supercritical nonlinear Schrödinger equation
  $i\partial_{t}u+Δu=|u|^p u$ in dimensions $d\ge5$. Killip-Visan [Comm. Partial Differential Equations, 2010] and Li-Li [Siam J. Math. Anal., 2022] proved that for $s_c:=\frac{d}{2}-\frac{2}{p}>1$, any solution that remains bounded in the critical Sobolev space $\dot H_x^{s_c}(\mathbb{R} ^d)$ must be global and scatter. In dimensions \(d \ge 8\), their results required either that \(p\) be even or that \(s_c < \frac{d+2-\sqrt{(d-2)^2-16}}{4}\). In this paper, we improve the upper bound on \(s_c\) to \(s_c<1+p\) by establishing some new nonlinear estimates. This allows us to cover all cases in which \(p\) lies in the local existence range.

</details>


### [55] [Nonexistence for effectively damped waves with time-dependent mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00953)
*Duc An Phan,The Anh Cung,Trung Loc Tang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the subcritical nonexistence of global weak solutions for semilinear wave equations with effective time-dependent damping and time-dependent mass, for η=1, complementing the supercritical existence results by D'Abbicco, Girardi and Reissig. The authors construct a positive slow adjoint mode under an accumulated-mass balance and Liouville nonoscillation condition, handling both subcritical and critical cases with Osgood-type divergence conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: D'Abbicco, Girardi and Reissig established global small-data existence in supercritical ranges and identified the critical exponent scale p_{β,η}(n). They proved a subcritical nonexistence result for the diffusion equation counterpart, but left the wave-equation case with effective damping and time-dependent mass open. This paper fills that gap for η=1.

Method: The authors prove nonexistence of global weak solutions by constructing a positive slow adjoint mode, under an intrinsic accumulated-mass balance condition and a Liouville nonoscillation condition. They treat the subcritical range 1<p<p_{β,1}(n) and the critical case p=p_{β,1}(n) using an Osgood divergence condition.

Result: They prove nonexistence of global weak solutions for 1<p<1+2/(n+2β) and treat the critical case p=1+2/(n+2β) under an Osgood divergence condition. Conditional lifespan upper bounds and explicit admissible coefficient families are also provided.

Conclusion: This work completes the sharpness analysis of the critical exponent p_{β,1}(n) for semilinear wave equations with time-dependent damping and mass, showing that the scale identified by D'Abbicco, Girardi and Reissig is indeed sharp for η=1 in the wave equation setting.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the semilinear wave equations $$ u_{tt}-Δu+b(t)u_t+m^2(t)u=|u|^p, \quad t \geq 0, \quad x\in\mathbb{R}^n $$ with effective time-dependent damping and a time-dependent mass dominated by the damping. D'Abbicco, Girardi and Reissig established global small-data existence in supercritical ranges and identified the scale $$
  p_{β,η}(n)=1+\frac{2η}{n+2ηβ} $$ for initial data in $(L^η(\mathbb R^n)\cap H^1(\mathbb R^n))\times(L^η(\mathbb R^n)\cap L^2(\mathbb R^n))$ with $1\leqη<2$, where $β$ is the lower mass index associated with the damping-mass pair. To support the expected sharpness of this scale, they also established an analogous subcritical nonexistence result for the corresponding diffusion equation with nonnegative initial data in $L^η(\mathbb{R}^n)$, leaving the wave-equation counterpart with effective damping and time-dependent mass open. We address this problem for $η=1$ under an intrinsic accumulated-mass balance and a Liouville nonoscillation condition. By constructing a positive slow adjoint mode, we prove nonexistence of global weak solutions for $$
  1<p<p_{β,1}(n)=1+\frac{2}{n+2β}, $$ and also treat the critical case $p=p_{β,1}(n)$ under an Osgood divergence condition. Conditional lifespan upper bounds and explicit admissible coefficient families are also given.

</details>


### [56] [Exponential decay for the 3D Boussinesq equations with Navier boundary conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00972)
*Wen Feng,Weinan Wang,Xiang Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs global weak solutions to the 3D incompressible Boussinesq equations on bounded domains with Navier boundary conditions via Galerkin approximation. It establishes exponential decay of energy for positive boundary friction, and partial exponential decay in the frictionless case. A key application resolves an open case for the Navier-Stokes system on solids of revolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to understand the global well-posedness and long-time asymptotic behavior of the Boussinesq equations—a fundamental model for geophysical and oceanographic flows—under physically relevant Navier (slip) boundary conditions, and to fill a gap left open in prior work by Kelliher (2025) regarding delay/decay results on solids of revolution.

Method: The proof combines a Galerkin approximation scheme to construct global weak solutions and Leray-Hopf energy inequalities, a weighted Korn-Poincaré inequality, and a two-time Gronwall-type inequality with an exponentially decaying forcing term to establish exponential decay rates.

Result: Global weak solutions with Leray-Hopf energy inequalities are obtained. For positive friction on a boundary subset of positive measure, total energy decays exponentially. In the frictionless case, the scalar field and the velocity component orthogonal to the rigid-motion kernel decay exponentially; when the kernel is trivial, total energy decays exponentially. This resolves the open case for Navier-Stokes on solids of revolution for any nonnegative, not-identically-zero friction coefficient.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete treatment of global existence and exponential energy decay for the Boussinesq equations under Navier boundary conditions, closing a previously open problem, via novel analytic tools (weighted Korn-Poincaré and two-time Gronwall inequalities).

Abstract: We study the three-dimensional incompressible Boussinesq equations on a bounded domain with smooth boundary and Navier boundary conditions. We construct global weak solutions by a Galerkin approximation and establish the associated Leray--Hopf energy inequalities. For nonnegative boundary friction, the total energy decays exponentially when the friction coefficient is positive on a boundary subset of positive surface measure. In the frictionless case, the scalar field and the velocity component orthogonal to the rigid-motion kernel decay exponentially; when the kernel is trivial, this is exponential decay of the total energy. When the scalar initial datum vanishes, this also proves exponential decay for the Navier--Stokes system on solids of revolution for every friction coefficient $α\in L^\infty(\partialΩ)$ such that $α\ge0$ almost everywhere and $α\not\equiv0$, resolving the corresponding case left open in \cite{Kelliher2025}. The proof uses a weighted Korn--Poincaré inequality and a two-time Gronwall-type inequality with an exponentially decaying forcing term.

</details>


### [57] [On the classification of invariant Gaussian measures for the 2D Euler equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01019)
*Ziyu Liu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves a classification of Gaussian invariant measures for the 2D incompressible Euler equation on the torus: such a measure is invariant if and only if it is supported on shear flows or cellular flows, resolving a conjecture by Bedrossian and Latocca.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize which Gaussian random initial data (with independent Fourier coefficients) yield invariant measures under the 2D Euler flow. This is important for understanding the ergodic/statistical behavior of the Euler equations and settles a longstanding open classification problem.

Method: The proof uses two key ideas: (1) closure of the Fourier support under non-degenerate (resonant) interactions, and (2) an affine relation among the inverse variances along Euler triples, which together constrain the admissible Gaussian measures.

Result: For every σ > 0, a Gaussian measure on H^σ(T²) with independent Fourier coefficients is invariant under the Euler flow if and only if it is supported on shear flows or cellular flows.

Conclusion: This completely classifies Gaussian invariant measures for the 2D Euler equation on the torus, confirming the conjecture of Bedrossian and Latocca.

Abstract: We consider the two-dimensional incompressible Euler equation on $\mathbb T^2$ with Gaussian random initial data having independent Fourier coefficients. For every $σ>0$, we prove that such a Gaussian measure on $H^σ(\mathbb T^2)$ is invariant under the Euler flow if and only if it is supported either on shear flows or on cellular flows. This settles the invariant-measure classification conjecture posed by Bedrossian and Latocca (Ann. Inst. H. Poincaré C Anal. Non Linéaire, 2026). The proof relies on closure of the Fourier support under non-degenerate interactions and an affine relation among the inverse variances along Euler triples.

</details>


### [58] [Sharp Boundary Recession Criteria for the Special Lagrangian Curvature Potential Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01065)
*Rongli Huang,Qinfeng Jiang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes boundary second derivative estimates for convex graphical solutions of the special Lagrangian curvature potential equation, introducing a mixed recession compatibility condition for degenerate level jets and deriving an exact complex Schur-complement identity for the double-normal derivative showing optimal 1/δ blow-up rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The special Lagrangian curvature potential equation's curvature matrix depends on both Du and D^2u, so a phase subsolution alone does not provide sufficient linearized separation for mixed derivative estimates. This necessitates a new compatibility condition.

Method: Introduces a mixed recession compatibility condition imposed only on doubly degenerate level jets to yield uniform mixed derivative bounds. For the double-normal derivative, employs an exact complex Schur-complement identity to derive the explicit formula u_νν = β + α·cot δ.

Result: The mixed derivative bound is sharp within the class of fixed smooth zero-order barriers. The double-normal derivative blows up if and only if the boundary limiting-phase gap δ collapses, with optimal rate δ^{-1}. Smooth radial solutions attain this rate, while a rank-loss model shows strict lower subsolutions need not force strict convexity.

Conclusion: The estimates are sharp, with curvature blow-up governed precisely by the boundary phase gap, and the mixed recession condition is the right tool for handling the dual dependence of the curvature matrix on gradient and Hessian.

Abstract: We establish boundary second derivative estimates for convex graphical solutions of the special Lagrangian curvature potential equation. Since the curvature matrix depends on both $Du$ and $D^2u$, a phase subsolution alone does not provide the full linearized separation needed for the mixed derivative estimate. We introduce a mixed recession compatibility condition imposed only on doubly degenerate level jets. It yields a uniform mixed derivative bound and is sharp within the class of fixed smooth zero-order barriers considered here. For the double-normal derivative, an exact complex Schur-complement identity gives \[
  u_{νν}=β+α\cotδ,
  \qquad 1\leqα\leq C,
  \qquad |β|\leq C, \] where $α$ and $β$ are explicit Schur-complement coefficients, $δ$ is the actual boundary limiting-phase gap, and $C$ depends only on uniform bounds for the gradient and the mixed boundary derivatives. Thus curvature blows up if and only if this gap collapses, with optimal rate $δ^{-1}$. Smooth radial solutions attain the rate, while a rank-loss model shows that a strict lower subsolution need not force strict convexity.

</details>


### [59] [The logarithmic $p$-Laplacian on hyperbolic spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01079)
*Jorge J. Betancor,Lourdes Rodríguez-Mesa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper introduces the logarithmic p-Laplacian operator on hyperbolic space ℍⁿ, proves a limiting result connecting it to the fractional p-Laplacian as s→0⁺, establishes a pointwise integral representation, and provides an extension theorem — including a novel result yielding the Euclidean logarithmic p-Laplacian that was previously unproven.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define and study the logarithmic p-Laplacian operator on hyperbolic space, establish its connection to the fractional p-Laplacian via a limiting process, and show that extension problem techniques can recover the Euclidean logarithmic p-Laplacian — a property not previously established.

Method: The paper introduces the logarithmic p-Laplacian operator log(−Δ_{ℍⁿ})_p on hyperbolic space, proves a limit result (s→0⁺) for the fractional p-Laplacian acting on locally Lipschitz functions with compact support, derives a pointwise integral representation for the operator, and constructs a suitable extension problem whose solution yields the operator, including the Euclidean case.

Result: Establishes that the fractional p-Laplacian limit as s→0⁺ gives A_{n,p}|f|^{p-2}f, provides a pointwise integral representation of the logarithmic p-Laplacian on hyperbolic space, and demonstrates the operator can be realized via an extension problem — achieving the Euclidean logarithmic p-Laplacian through this framework, which was not previously known.

Conclusion: The logarithmic p-Laplacian on hyperbolic space is rigorously well-defined with desirable limit and representation properties, and the extension problem approach successfully covers both hyperbolic and Euclidean settings.

Abstract: In this paper, the logarithmic $p$-Laplacian operator $\log (-Δ_{\mathbb H ^n})_p$ on the hyperbolic space $\mathbb H^n$, with $n\geq 2$, is introduced. We prove that if $f$ is a locally Lipschitz function of exponent $α\in (0,1)$ with compact support in $\mathbb H^n$, then, for a suitable constant $A_{n,p}>0$, $$ \lim_{s\rightarrow 0^+}(-Δ_{\mathbb H ^n})_p^sf(x)=A_{n,p}|f(x)|^{p-2}f(x),\quad x\in \mathbb H^n, $$ where $(-Δ_{\mathbb H ^n})_p^s$ denotes the $s$-fractional $p$-Laplacian on $\mathbb H^n$. We establish a pointwise integral representation for the operator $\log (-Δ_{\mathbb H ^n})_p=\frac{d}{ds}(-Δ_{\mathbb H^n})_p^s\,_{|s=0}$. Furthermore, we show that $\log (-Δ_{\mathbb H ^n})_p$ can be realized as the solution of a suitable extension problem and provide an extension theorem that yields the operator $\log (-Δ)_p$ in $\mathbb R^n$. To the best of our knowledge, this property has not been established for the Euclidean logarithmic $p$-Laplacian $\log (-Δ)_p$.

</details>


### [60] [Uniqueness and boundary behaviour of solutions to variational problems with linear growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01105)
*Michael Bildhauer,Martin Fuchs*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the Dirichlet problem for a variational integral with linear-growth density, proving existence and uniqueness of a relaxed solution in BV space when a sufficiently large portion of the boundary consists of convex points. The minimizer is smooth in the interior and achieves boundary data classically on the convex boundary set.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Variational problems with linear growth densities often lack classical solutions and require relaxed formulations in BV spaces. A key challenge is ensuring existence and uniqueness of the relaxed solution, which depends on the geometry of the boundary — specifically whether enough of the boundary is convex. This paper aims to establish sharp sufficient conditions on the convex portion of the boundary.

Method: The approach involves studying the relaxed (lower semicontinuous envelope) variational problem in the space BV(Ω). The sufficient condition on the convex boundary portion Γ₀ is expressed via a measure-theoretic inequality involving the Hausdorff measure of Γ₀ relative to the boundary. Interior regularity is established via standard elliptic methods applicable to the minimizer.

Result: The relaxed problem has a unique solution u ∈ BV(Ω) provided the Hausdorff measure of the convex boundary set Γ₀ is sufficiently large — e.g., H^{n-1}(Γ₀) > (2/3)H^{n-1}(∂Ω) suffices. The minimizer is smooth in the interior of Ω and attains boundary data classically on Γ₀.

Conclusion: The paper provides concrete geometric conditions on the boundary that guarantee well-posedness of the relaxed Dirichlet problem for linear-growth variational integrals, along with interior regularity and partial boundary attainment of the data.

Abstract: We investigate the Dirichlet problem for the variational integral $J[u] = \int_Ω f(\nabla u) \, dx$ with density $f$ of linear growth satisfying appropriate ellipticity conditions. We show that the relaxed problem admits a unique solution $u$ in the space of functions of bounded variation, if the set $Γ_0$ of convex points $x \in \partialΩ$ is sufficiently large. For example, the inequality $\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Γ_0) > \frac{2}{3}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partialΩ)$ is sufficient. Moreover, the minimizer $u$ is smooth in the interior of $Ω$ and attains the prescribed boundary data at least on $Γ_0$ in the classical sense.

</details>


### [61] [New definitions of decay indicators and critical exponent for fractional semi-linear structurally damped evolution equations on the Heisenberg group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01132)
*Duc An Phan,The Anh Cung*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper introduces lower and upper decay indicators and decay characters on the Heisenberg group to derive optimal decay estimates for linear and semilinear fractional damped wave/plate equations. The authors establish global existence and decay in the subcritical and critical cases for the semilinear problem, and derive blow-up results with critical exponents via test functions adapted to nonlocal fractional sub-Laplacians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a framework for characterizing the decay of initial data in function spaces on the Heisenberg group, and to use this to obtain sharp decay estimates for fractional diffusion and damped wave/plate equations, extending existing Euclidean-space results to the non-commutative Heisenberg group setting.

Method: The authors introduce decay indicators and decay characters on the Heisenberg group, use them to derive linear decay estimates in homogeneous fractional Sobolev spaces, then analyze the semilinear Cauchy problem with power nonlinearity |u|^p using energy methods and decay character techniques. For blow-up, they construct test functions adapted to nonlocal fractional sub-Laplacians.

Result: Global existence and decay are proved for p above a critical exponent involving the Heisenberg group dimension, decay characters, and fractional parameters, including the critical case. Blow-up results and the critical exponent are identified via test function arguments for initial data in negative-order homogeneous Sobolev spaces on the Heisenberg group.

Conclusion: The results recover known estimates on the Heisenberg group and extend them to new classes of initial data characterized by decay characters, providing a unified theory for linear and semilinear fractional damped wave/plate equations on the Heisenberg group.

Abstract: In this paper, we introduce the lower and upper decay indicators and the associated decay character on the Heisenberg group. These notions are used to derive decay estimates for the linear fractional diffusion equation and to characterize the decay of initial data in several function spaces. We then study the Cauchy problem $$ \partial_t^2u+\left(-Δ{\mathrm H}\right)^{δ_1}u+\left(-Δ_{\mathrm H}\right)^{δ_2}\partial_tu=0, \quad δ_1\in\left[0,\frac{δ_2}{2}\right], $$ and establish decay estimates for solutions and their derivatives in homogeneous fractional Sobolev spaces in terms of the decay characters of the initial data. These results recover known estimates and extend them to new classes of data. We also investigate the corresponding semilinear problem with nonlinearity $|u|^p$. Global existence and decay are proved for $$ p>1+\frac{2ωδ_1}{Q-2ωδ_2}, \quad ω=\frac{Q}{Q+\min{r_{\mathrm H}(u_0),r_{\mathrm H}(u_1)-2δ_2}+2δ_2}, $$ together with the corresponding critical case. Finally, by constructing test functions adapted to the nonlocal fractional sub-Laplacians, we establish blow-up results and identify the critical exponent $$ p=1+\frac{2mδ_1}{Q+mγ-2mδ_2} $$ for initial data in $\dot H_m^{-γ}(\mathbf H_n)$, where $m\in(1,2]$ and $γ\in\left[0,Q-\frac{Q}{m}\right)$.

</details>


### [62] [Small-data $L^2$ theory for the intermediate NLS and the Calogero--Moser derivative NLS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01138)
*Sonae Hadama*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified perturbative approach to study a class of nonlinear Schrödinger equations (NLS) including intermediate NLS (INLS) and Calogero-Moser derivative NLS (CM-DNLS), proving small-data global well-posedness in L²(ℝ) and scattering under additional assumptions, using rough time-dependent potential theory and Ozawa-Tsutsumi's bilinear Strichartz estimate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified framework for analyzing a broad class of nonlinear Schrödinger equations that includes important examples like INLS and CM-DNLS, without relying on integrability, and to prove well-posedness and scattering results that cover arbitrary parameter choices for these equations.

Method: The approach is perturbative, built on the bilinear Strichartz estimate by Ozawa and Tsutsumi (1998). It relies crucially on a linear theory for Schrödinger equations with rough time-dependent potentials—so rough that the standard Duhamel formulation may not even be well-defined—which is developed and used both for the formulation of the problem (including the notion of solution) and for the proofs.

Result: First, small-data global well-posedness in L²(ℝ) is proved for a broad class of equations, covering both focusing and defocusing CM-DNLS and INLS for arbitrary parameter choices. Second, small-data scattering in L²(ℝ) is proved under an additional assumption, covering both focusing and defocusing CM-DNLS and INLS for specific parameter choices.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a unified, non-integrable perturbative framework for a broad class of NLS equations, providing global well-posedness and scattering results, while also introducing a linear theory for Schrödinger equations with rough time-dependent potentials that is of independent interest.

Abstract: In this paper, we study a class of nonlinear Schrödinger equations (NLS) in a unified way. This class includes two important examples: the intermediate NLS (INLS) and the Calogero--Moser derivative NLS (CM-DNLS). Our main results are twofold. First, we prove small-data global well-posedness in $L^2(\mathbb{R})$ for a broad class of equations. This includes both focusing and defocusing CM-DNLS and the INLS for arbitrary choices of its parameters. Second, we prove small-data scattering in $L^2(\mathbb{R})$ under an additional assumption. This result covers both focusing and defocusing CM-DNLS and the INLS for specific choices of its parameters. Both the formulation of the problem, including the notion of solution, and the proofs rely crucially on a linear theory for Schrödinger equations with rough time-dependent potentials. This theory is also of independent interest, since we allow potentials so rough that the standard Duhamel formulation may not make sense. Our approach is perturbative and is built on the bilinear Strichartz estimate proved by Ozawa and Tsutsumi in 1998. In particular, our argument does not rely on integrability.

</details>


### [63] [Wasserstein gradient flows of Maximum Mean Discrepancy with energy kernels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01182)
*Matthew Rosenzweig,Dejan Slepčev,Lihan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies Wasserstein gradient flows of squared MMD with nonsmooth energy kernels K(z)=-|z|^q (0<q<2) in dimensions d≥2, proving global well-posedness, particle-system convergence, rigidity results, and negative decay/PL-inequality results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The squared MMD with nonsmooth energy kernels lacks displacement semiconvexity in dimensions d≥2, so standard Wasserstein gradient flow theory does not apply. The authors aim to develop a well-posedness and convergence theory for these energies despite this obstruction, covering the one-dimensional Coulomb endpoint as well.

Method: The analysis uses subcritical L^p spaces for global well-posedness on R^d. For the N-particle system, they employ noncollision proofs, fixed-N convergence to collision-free critical sets, a particle-to-continuum criticality principle, and modulated-energy mean-field estimates. Lagrangian critical set analysis and rigidity theorems (finite moment conditions) are used for convergence to targets.

Result: Global well-posedness is proven for d+q-2>0 and the 1D Coulomb endpoint. Particle systems converge to the continuum flow as N→∞ on finite intervals. For 1≤q<2, solutions have relatively compact orbits converging to Lagrangian critical sets; for 0<q<1 this holds under uniform-in-time bounds. Lagrangian critical points equal targets except for 0<q<1, d∈{1,3}. No initial-data-independent MMD decay modulus exists, and global PL inequalities fail in several regimes.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a comprehensive well-posedness, convergence, and rigidity theory for MMD gradient flows with nonsmooth energy kernels, while identifying fundamental limitations (no uniform decay, PL inequality failures) and exceptional rergimes (0<q<1, d∈{1,3}) where rigidity may fail.

Abstract: We study the Wasserstein gradient flow of the squared Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) generated by the nonsmooth energy kernels $K(z)=-|z|^q$, $0<q<2$. In dimensions $d\ge2$, the corresponding energies are not displacement semiconvex, so standard Wasserstein-gradient-flow theory does not apply. When $d+q-2>0$, we prove global well-posedness on $\mathbb{R}^d$ for probability densities in subcritical $L^p$ spaces, with targets in the same integrability class and with finite moments. We also include the one-dimensional Coulomb endpoint $d=q=1$.
  For the associated $N$-particle system, we prove global noncollision and fixed-$N$ convergence to the collision-free critical set, a particle-to-continuum criticality principle, and a modulated-energy mean-field estimate that yields convergence of the particle dynamics to the continuum flow as $N\to\infty$ on every finite time interval. We also construct collision-free saddle equilibria, showing that deterministic particle trajectories need not approach global empirical minimizers.
  For $1\le q<2$, every continuum solution in our class has a narrowly relatively compact orbit, every $ω$-limit point is Lagrangian critical, and the orbit approaches the Lagrangian critical set. For $0<q<1$, the same conclusions hold under uniform-in-time moment and subcritical $L^p$ bounds. We prove that an absolutely continuous Lagrangian critical point equals the target when the source and target have finite moments of order $q$, except when $0<q<1$ and $d\in\{1,3\}$. Under the preceding uniform bounds, rigidity gives convergence of the continuum flow to the target throughout the rigid part of the well-posedness range.
  Finally, we show that no initial-data-independent multiplicative MMD decay modulus exists on $\mathbb{R}^d$, and that global Polyak--Łojasiewicz inequalities fail in several whole-space and periodic Riesz/Coulomb regimes.

</details>


### [64] [A semiconvex counterexample to energy monotonicity for time-fractional gradient flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01188)
*Marvin Fritz*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that for time-fractional gradient flows, the original energy can strictly increase along a solution trajectory, contrary to typical dissipation expectations. The authors construct a smooth, compactly supported, globally semiconvex energy and an absolutely continuous solution on a finite interval where this anomalous behavior occurs explicitly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: For time-fractional gradient flows, classical energy dissipation is not expressed as pointwise differential inequalities for the original energy—instead, dissipation statements are integrated or memory-modified. The paper aims to investigate whether the original energy can actually increase along trajectories, questioning the extent to which standard dissipation intuition carries over to the fractional setting.

Method: The construction embeds the initial layer directly into the trajectory. After transformation, the curve and its fractional derivative become polynomial, the curve is a regular immersion, and the Hessian remains bounded below up to the anchoring point—enabling explicit verification of the semiconvex energy condition.

Result: The authors explicitly construct an energy and a solution where the original energy is strictly increasing on an explicit subinterval, demonstrating that pointwise energy monotonicity fails in time-fractional gradient flows even for nice (smooth, compactly supported, globally semiconvex) energies.

Conclusion: The original energy of a time-fractional gradient flow can increase along trajectories, confirming that only integrated or memory-modified dissipation statements are valid, and pointwise monotonicity of the standard energy is not guaranteed in this setting.

Abstract: For time-fractional gradient flows, natural dissipation statements are often integrated or memory-modified rather than pointwise differential inequalities for the original energy. We construct a smooth, compactly supported, globally semiconvex energy and an absolutely continuous solution on a finite time interval of a time-fractional gradient flow along which the original energy is strictly increasing on an explicit subinterval. The construction incorporates the initial layer directly into the trajectory. After transformation, the curve and its fractional derivative are polynomial, the curve is a regular immersion, and the Hessian remains bounded below up to the anchor.

</details>


### [65] [A sharp integral criterion for the Lane--Emden system of inequalities on weighted graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01191)
*Qingsong Gu,Lu Hao,Xueping Huang,Yuhua Sun*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a sharp integral nonexistence (Liouville-type) criterion for the Lane-Emden system of inequalities on arbitrary infinite connected locally finite weighted graphs, proving that under a summability condition on the graph volume growth, every nonnegative solution must be identically zero. The asymmetric case is handled via flow decomposition of the finite Green current combined with nonlinear testing, and the symmetric case reduces to a scalar criterion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the classical Liouville theorem for Lane-Emden systems from Euclidean/continuous settings to the general discrete setting of weighted graphs, providing sharp conditions on graph volume growth under which no positive solutions exist.

Method: The proof combines flow decomposition of the finite Green current with nonlinear testing for the asymmetric case p≠q. For the symmetric case p=q>1, the problem reduces via the sum u+v to a scalar Liouville criterion.

Result: A sharp integral criterion is established: if ∑ n^{2pq+2P-1}/μ(B(o,n))^{pq-1} = ∞ (asymmetric) or ∑ n^{2p-1}/μ(B(o,n))^{p-1} = ∞ (symmetric), then every nonnegative solution is trivial. Weighted half-line examples demonstrate sharpness of the critical logarithmic endpoint.

Conclusion: The nonexistence criterion is sharp, and the critical logarithmic endpoint in the asymmetric result is precisely characterized by half-line examples.

Abstract: We establish a sharp integral nonexistence criterion for the Lane--Emden system of inequalities
  \[
  -Δu\ge v^p,\qquad -Δv\ge u^q,
  \qquad p,q>0,\quad pq>1,
  \]
  on arbitrary infinite, connected, locally finite weighted graphs. In the
  asymmetric case $p\ne q$, set $P=\max\{p,q\}$. If, for some root $o\in V$,
  \[
  \sum_{n=2}^{\infty}
  \frac{n^{2pq+2P-1}}{μ(B(o,n))^{pq-1}}=\infty,
  \] then every nonnegative solution $(u,v)$ satisfies $u\equiv v\equiv0$. The proof combines flow decomposition of the finite Green current with nonlinear testing. In the symmetric case $p=q>1$, the Liouville problem reduces, via the sum $u+v$, to the scalar criterion
  \[
  \sum_{n=2}^{\infty}
  \frac{n^{2p-1}}{μ(B(o,n))^{p-1}}=\infty.
  \] Weighted half-line examples show that the critical logarithmic endpoint in the asymmetric result is sharp.

</details>


### [66] [Global $L^p$ Second Commutation Lemma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01216)
*Marin Mišur*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper extends Tartar's second commutation lemma from L² theory to global L^p spaces (1 < p < ∞) on unbounded domains, using Calderón–Zygmund kernel estimates, a Taylor-remainder identity, and spatial truncations to overcome the lack of Hilbert space tools. It derives L^p transport equations, establishes a phase-space bicharacteristic flow (Vlasov equation) for first-order scalar PDEs, and lifts the local energy identity to a microlocal Poynting-flux transport for the quasilinear p-Wave system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to generalize the second commutation lemma from the L² Hilbert space setting to the global L^p Banach space framework of H-distributions, since the L² theory relies on Plancherel isometry and Hilbert compactness tools that are unavailable in L^p on unbounded domains. This is needed to track propagation of singularities in degenerate nonlinear and fractional PDEs.

Method: The proof resolves the non-local tail problem via elementary Calderón–Zygmund kernel estimates, an explicit Taylor-remainder identity, and spatial truncations. The lemma is then applied by pairing macroscopic sequences with canonical Nemyckij duals to derive L^p transport equations, yielding the Vlasov (bicharacteristic flow) equation for first-order scalar PDEs (p ≥ 2) and microlocal energy transport for the quasilinear p-Wave system.

Result: The second commutation lemma is proven for L^p(ℝ^d) with 1 < p < ∞ on the global unbounded domain. For first-order scalar PDEs with p ≥ 2, the phase-space bicharacteristic flow (Vlasov equation) is established. For the quasilinear p-Wave system, the local energy identity is lifted to a microlocal Poynting-flux transport in the linear core (p = 2), while for p ≠ 2 a structural obstruction to its closure is isolated.

Conclusion: The results provide new functional analytic tools for tracking singularity propagation in degenerate nonlinear and fractional PDEs, extending H-distribution theory beyond the Hilbert space setting to the global L^p framework.

Abstract: We prove the second commutation lemma for Lebesgue spaces $L^p(\R^d)$ ($1 < p < \infty$) on the global unbounded domain, extending the $L^2$ theory of Tartar \cite{Tartar1990} to the Banach space framework of H-distributions. Unlike the $L^2$ setting, where the Plancherel isometry and the compactness tools of Hilbert space are available (as in the parabolic variants of Antonić and Lazar \cite{AntonicLazar2013}), the global $L^p$ setting lacks both. We resolve the non-local tail problem through elementary Calderón--Zygmund kernel estimates, an explicit Taylor-remainder identity, and spatial truncations. Furthermore, by pairing macroscopic sequences with their canonical Nemyckij duals, we use the lemma to derive $L^p$ transport equations. We establish the phase-space bicharacteristic flow (Vlasov equation) for first-order scalar PDEs (for $p \ge 2$), and for the quasilinear $p$-Wave system we lift the local energy identity to the microlocal level, obtaining a Poynting-flux transport of microlocal energy in the linear core ($p = 2$) and isolating the structural obstruction to its closure when $p \neq 2$. These results provide functional analytic tools for tracking the propagation of singularities in degenerate nonlinear and fractional partial differential equations.

</details>


### [67] [The continuity properties of the solutions of the wave equation at different boundary conditions across the characteristic line](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01229)
*Alemdar Hasanov,Vladimir G. Romanov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the continuity properties of wave equation solutions on characteristic lines under Dirichlet, Neumann, and Robin boundary conditions, using d'Alembert's formula to derive and compare solution behaviors across these three boundary condition types.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The continuity and singularity propagation of wave equation solutions depend crucially on boundary conditions and their interaction with characteristic lines. Understanding how different boundary conditions (Dirichlet, Neumann, Robin) affect solution behavior on characteristics is fundamental to properly analyzing wave propagation problems.

Method: The study employs d'Alembert's formula to obtain explicit solution representations for the wave equation and examines their behavior along characteristic lines under three types of boundary conditions: Dirichlet, Neumann, and Robin.

Result: The paper provides formulas for wave equation solutions on characteristic lines and analyzes how continuity properties and singularity behavior differ across Dirichlet, Neumann, and Robin boundary conditions.

Conclusion: The behavior of wave equation solutions on characteristic lines is strongly determined by the type of boundary condition imposed, with each of the three boundary condition types producing distinct continuity and singularity propagation characteristics.

Abstract: It is well-known the continuity properties of solutions to the wave equation depend strongly on both the initial data and the boundary conditions, particularly across the characteristic lines, where information propagates. Moreover, the continuity properties of solutions to the wave equation depend strongly on the type of boundary condition imposed and on the interaction of the characteristics with the boundary. Across the characteristic lines, singularities and discontinuities propagate according to the geometry of the problem, while the boundary conditions determine how these singularities are re?ected or transmitted. In this study, we will examine the behavior of solutions of the wave equation, corresponding to different boundary conditions, on its characteristic line, using the formulas obtained as a result of d'Alembert's formula, for all the three, Dirichlet, Neumann and Robin, types of boundary conditions.

</details>


### [68] [Optimal data-driven solutions for a stationary diffusive model of population growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01242)
*Laura Baldelli,Paolo Malanchini,Wolfgang Reichel*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies optimal data-driven solutions for the stationary diffusive population growth model -Δu = r u with Neumann boundary conditions, characterizing the relaxed cost functional via partial lower convex envelopes and proving existence, consistency, and Γ-convergence of optimal solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Instead of prescribing a functional relation between position x, growth rate r, and population size u, the authors aim to find pairs (u,r) that optimally fit given data sets, addressing scenarios where explicit functional relations may be unknown or incomplete.

Method: The authors formulate an optimization problem with a cost functional I and penalty term. They characterize the relaxed cost functional sc⁻I by showing its density is the partial lower convex envelope with respect to r, then prove existence of optimal solutions, establish consistency with conventional solutions, and use Γ-convergence for evolving data sets.

Result: The relaxed cost functional's density is characterized as the partial lower convex envelope in r; existence of optimal data-driven solutions is proven; a consistency result links conventional solutions -Δu = ρ(x,u)u to data-driven ones; Γ-convergence of cost functionals ensures convergence of optimal solutions as data sets evolve.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous mathematical framework for data-driven population growth models, ensuring well-posedness, consistency with classical models, and stability under data evolution — all essential for reliable application to real population data.

Abstract: We study optimal data-driven solutions for the stationary diffusive population growth model $-Δu = r u$ in a bounded domain $Ω\subset\mathbb R^N$ with Neumann boundary conditions. Instead of prescribing a functional relation between the position $x$, the net per-capita growth rate $r$ and the population size $u$, we look for a pair $(u,r)\in H^1(Ω)\times L^\infty(Ω)$ that fits a given data set in an optimal way measured by a cost functional $I$ and an additional penalty term. We characterize the relaxed cost functional sc$^- I$ by showing that its density is given as the partial lower convex envelope with respect to the variable $r$, and prove the existence of optimal data-driven solutions. Furthermore, we establish a consistency result comparing conventional solutions of $-Δu = \varrho(x,u)u$ with optimal data-driven solutions where the data set stems from the functional relation $(x,u)\mapsto \varrho(x,u)$. Finally, as data sets evolve, we prove the convergence of optimal solutions via the $Γ$-convergence of the associated cost functionals.

</details>


### [69] [Existence of multiple solutions for the generalized Abelian Chern-Simons-Higgs model on a torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01275)
*Jongmin Han,Kyungwoo Song*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs multiple solutions to the generalized self-dual Abelian Chern-Simons-Higgs equation on a two-dimensional flat torus using the topological degree method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to establish the existence of multiple solutions for a generalized self-dual Abelian Chern-Simons-Higgs equation, extending previous results that typically showed a single solution.

Method: The authors employ the topological degree method on a two-dimensional flat torus to construct multiple solutions to the equation.

Result: Multiple solutions to the generalized self-dual Abelian Chern-Simons-Higgs equation are constructed on the flat torus.

Conclusion: The topological degree method successfully yields multiple solutions for the generalized self-dual Abelian Chern-Simons-Higgs equation in the toroidal setting.

Abstract: We construct multiple solutions of the generalized self-dual Abelian Chern-Simons-Higgs equation in a two dimensional flat torus by the topological degree method.

</details>


### [70] [Complete Spectrum and Sharp Local Stability for the Critical Exponential Biharmonic Choquard Equation in \(\mathbb R^{4}\)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01380)
*Wenjing Chen,Shengbing Deng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper provides the complete spectral resolution of the linearized operator for the conformally invariant exponential biharmonic Choquard equation on R^4, showing the Morse index is one, the kernel is the five-dimensional conformal space, and all higher modes satisfy uniform coercivity. The spectrum yields sharp local stability results and the optimal asymptotic constant is computed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully understand the local stability and spectral properties of the conformally invariant exponential biharmonic Choquard equation, which requires resolving the linearized operator at its conformal bubbles—a task complicated by distributional defects and the non-trivial structure of the Paneitz operator.

Method: The authors stereographically project the operator to become a bounded zeroth-order perturbation of the Paneitz operator on the sphere S^4, justify a weak conformal transfer, remove pole-supported distributional defects, and compute all eigenvalues explicitly. They also prove that all normal distributional finite-mass solutions satisfy Niu's classification theorem hypotheses.

Result: The Morse index is one, the kernel is the five-dimensional conformal space, and all higher modes satisfy uniform coercivity. The optimal asymptotic constant is γ_α = (160-12α-α²)/(40(10-α)), minimizing the limiting quotient precisely at the second spherical harmonics.

Conclusion: The complete spectrum establishes sharp local stability with respect to the Paneitz distance from the conformal extremal manifold, and all finite-mass distributional solutions are explicit translation-dilation bubbles.

Abstract: We study the conformally invariant exponential biharmonic Choquard equation in \(\mathbb R^{4}\). Our principal result is the complete spectral resolution of the linearized operator at its conformal bubbles. After stereographic projection, the operator becomes a bounded zeroth-order perturbation of the Paneitz operator, with a compact Riesz component on \(L^{2}(\mathbb S^{4})\). We justify the weak conformal transfer, remove every pole-supported distributional defect, and compute all eigenvalues. The Morse index is one. The kernel is the five-dimensional conformal space. All higher modes satisfy a uniform coercivity estimate. The transverse Hessian of the Adams--Choquard deficit is the same linearized operator. Hence the complete spectrum gives sharp local stability with respect to the Paneitz distance from the conformal extremal manifold. The optimal asymptotic constant is \[ γ_α =\frac{160-12α-α^{2}}{40(10-α)}, \] and the limiting quotient is minimized precisely by the second spherical harmonics. As a nonlinear preparation, we also prove that every normal distributional finite-mass solution satisfies the hypotheses of Niu's classification theorem and is therefore an explicit translation--dilation bubble.

</details>


### [71] [Bernstein's theorem for variational integrals of linear growth and radial structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01435)
*Martin fuchs,Michael Bildhauer*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves that for entire solutions of the Euler-Lagrange equation of a variational integral with a strictly convex density of linear growth, the finiteness condition ∫₀^∞ t·g''(t)dt < ∞ implies the Bernstein property (all solutions must be affine). Weaker conditions yield partial Bernstein results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Bernstein property—whether all entire solutions to a variational problem must be affine—is a fundamental question in the calculus of variations. For densities of linear growth (such as those appearing in minimal surface-type and anisotropic problems), establishing this property requires specific structural assumptions on the convex density g, and the paper aims to determine sharp sufficient conditions.

Method: The paper analyzes the Euler-Lagrange equation associated with the variational integral ∫ g(|∇u|) dx for strictly convex densities g of linear growth. The key condition investigated is the integrability of t·g''(t) over [0,∞). Under this condition, the Bernstein property is proven. When the condition is relaxed, the authors establish partial Bernstein results by weakening the global conclusion.

Result: The condition ∫₀^∞ t·g''(t)dt < ∞ is sufficient to guarantee the Bernstein property for entire solutions u: ℝ²→ℝ. If this condition fails (i.e., is weakened), the authors still obtain partial Bernstein-type results rather than a complete failure of the property.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a sharp integrability condition on the second derivative of the density g that ensures the Bernstein property for variational problems with linear growth, and provides weaker fallback results when this condition does not hold.

Abstract: We consider entire solutions $u: \mathbb{R}^2 \rightarrow \mathbb{R}$ of the Euler-Lagrange equation associated to the variational integral $\int_Ω g(|\nabla u|)\,dx$ with a strictly convex density $g: [0,\infty)\rightarrow \mathbb{R}$ being of linear growth. We show that the condition $\int_{0}^{\infty} t\,g''(t)\,dt < \infty$ implies the Bernstein property, which means that $u$ must be an affine function. If this condition on g is weakened, we still have some partial Bernstein results.

</details>


### [72] [Local smoothing for rough wave equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01440)
*Jan Rozendaal,Robert Schippa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes local smoothing estimates for wave equations with C^r coefficients using bilinear restriction estimates for rough wave propagation. For C^{1,1} coefficients in 2D and 3D, the authors recover sharp L^p-L^q local smoothing bounds previously known for smooth coefficients, via stronger L^p-L^q decoupling inequalities with endpoint derivative loss, and derive non-trivial L^p-L^p local smoothing estimates as a corollary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend sharp local smoothing estimates for wave equations from smooth coefficients to rough (C^r) coefficients, which is more realistic for applications where the medium is not perfectly smooth.

Method: The approach uses bilinear restriction estimates adapted to rough wave propagation, and derives L^p-L^q decoupling inequalities with endpoint derivative loss to imply the desired local smoothing bounds.

Result: In two and three dimensions with C^{1,1} coefficients, sharp L^p-L^q local smoothing bounds matching those for smooth coefficients are recovered, plus non-trivial L^p-L^p local smoothing estimates as a corollary.

Conclusion: Sharp local smoothing estimates hold even for rough (C^{1,1}) coefficients in 2D and 3D, demonstrating the robustness of these bounds beyond the smooth setting.

Abstract: We prove local smoothing estimates for wave equations with $C^{r}$ coefficients, relying on bilinear restriction estimates associated with rough wave propagation. In two and three dimensions, for $C^{1,1}$ coefficients we recover the sharp $L^p$-$L^q$ local smoothing bounds previously established for smooth coefficients. These are implied by stronger $L^{p}$-$L^{q}$ decoupling inequalities with endpoint derivative loss. As a corollary, we obtain non-trivial $L^{p}$-$L^{p}$ local smoothing estimates as well.

</details>


### [73] [Fully Nonlinear Evolution equations: From Critical Integrability to Schauder Estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01455)
*Junior da Silva Bessa,José Erivamberto L. Oliveira,Patrícia Renata Pereira Regis*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops sharp boundary regularity theory for viscosity solutions of fully nonlinear parabolic equations with oblique derivative boundary conditions, establishing Log-Lipschitz continuity in the borderline case f∈L^{n+2}, optimal C^{1+α'} estimates when f∈L^p for p>n+2, and Schauder-type estimates at higher regularity levels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish sharp (optimal) boundary regularity results for viscosity solutions of fully nonlinear parabolic equations with oblique derivative boundary conditions, which had not been fully developed across different integrability regimes of the source term.

Method: A compactness method combined with polynomial approximation, adapted to the integrability of the source term, smoothness of boundary data, and oscillation of the operator's coefficients.

Result: Log-Lipschitz continuity up to the boundary in the borderline case f∈L^{n+2}; optimal C^{1+α',(1+α')/2} boundary estimates for f∈L^p (p>n+2); Schauder-type estimates at higher regularity; parabolic C^{1,Log-Lip} regularity in the critical BMO case as a byproduct.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete and sharp boundary regularity theory for fully nonlinear parabolic equations with oblique boundary conditions, covering the full spectrum from borderline L^{n+2} integrability to Schauder estimates.

Abstract: We investigate the sharp regularity up to the boundary for viscosity solutions of fully nonlinear parabolic equations with oblique derivative boundary conditions given by \begin{equation*} \left\{ \begin{array}{rclcl} F(D^2u,x,t) - u_{t} &=& f(x,t) & \text{in} & Q_{1}^{+}, \\ β(x,t) \cdot Du &=& g(x,t) & \text{on} & Q_{1}^{*}. \end{array} \right. \end{equation*} The regularity theory is developed according to the integrability of the source term, the smoothness of the boundary data, and the oscillation of the coefficients of the operator \(F\), using a compactness method combined with polynomial approximation. In the borderline case \(f\in L^{n+2}\), we obtain Log-Lipschitz continuity of solutions up to the boundary. Under stronger integrability, specifically when \(f\in L^{p}\) for some \(p>n+2\), we establish optimal \(C^{1+α',\,\frac{1+α'}{2}}\) boundary estimates. At a higher regularity level, we prove Schauder-type estimates under appropriate assumptions on the operator \(F\) and the boundary data. As a byproduct, we obtain parabolic \(C^{1,\mathrm{Log\text{-}Lip}}\) regularity in the critical borderline case where the source term belongs to BMO space.

</details>


### [74] [Free Boundary Regularity for Non-Convex Fully Nonlinear Alt-Phillips Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01508)
*Alvis Zahl*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes that flat free boundaries are smooth for the fully nonlinear Alt-Phillips free boundary problem F(D²u) = u^γ χ_{u>0} for γ ∈ (-1/3, 1), without any convexity assumption on F. This is achieved via a partial hodograph transform, resulting in a fully nonlinear degenerate equation for which new Harnack inequality and Schauder estimates are proven.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fully nonlinear Alt-Phillips free boundary problem has previously only been studied with convexity assumptions on F. The authors aim to remove this restriction and establish regularity of flat free boundaries, which is new even for the fully nonlinear obstacle problem (γ=0).

Method: The approach uses a partial hodograph transform to convert the free boundary into a fixed boundary, yielding a fully nonlinear degenerate equation. For this transformed equation, the authors prove a Harnack inequality that produces improvement of flatness, and a Schauder estimate for the associated linearized equation.

Result: Flat free boundaries are proven to be smooth for the fully nonlinear Alt-Phillips problem without convexity assumptions on F. This result covers γ ∈ (-1/3, 1) and is new even for γ=0 (the fully nonlinear obstacle problem).

Conclusion: The paper provides a novel framework via the partial hodograph transform to handle the nonconvex fully nonlinear setting, with Harnack and Schauder estimates that are of independent interest beyond this specific problem.

Abstract: This paper studies the fully nonlinear Alt-Phillips free boundary problem \begin{equation} F(D^2u) = u^γχ_{\{u>0\}}, \end{equation} for $γ\in (-1/3,1)$ without any convexity assumption on $F$. We establish that flat free boundaries are smooth. This result is new even for the fully nonlinear obstacle problem when $γ= 0$.
  Our approach is based on a partial hodograph transform, which converts the free boundary into a fixed boundary and produces a fully nonlinear degenerate equation. For this equation we prove a Harnack inequality, yielding an improvement of flatness, and a Schauder estimate for the associated linearized equation. Both estimates appear to be new and are of independent interest.

</details>


### [75] [A computer-assisted counterexample to the planar Pompeiu and Schiffer conjectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01579)
*Matthew J. Colbrook,George Stepaniants*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs an explicit bounded, simply connected, noncircular domain with real-analytic boundary that serves as a counterexample to both Schiffer's conjecture and the planar Pompeiu conjecture. The domain is obtained via a ten-fold symmetric conformal map close to a degree-301 polynomial, derived by solving a cubic operator equation through a posteriori contraction arguments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The planar Pompeiu problem (1929) and Schiffer conjecture are long-standing rigidity questions connecting rigid-motion integral transforms and Fourier zero sets to overdetermined Neumann eigenvalue problems. The question of whether such noncircular counterexamples exist has remained open for nearly a century.

Method: The authors construct the domain as Ω=φ(𝔻) via a ten-fold symmetric conformal map near an explicitly listed degree-301 polynomial. On the unit disc, the problem reduces to a cubic operator equation F(g,p)=g+|p|²(1+Kg)=0 on real coefficient spaces, where K is an explicit inverse Laplacian. Positivity of linearisation coefficients, sharp bounds for K, and monotone tail control establish a posteriori contraction in a weighted coefficient algebra, proving an exact zero exists.

Result: A nonconstant function u satisfying (Δ+k²)u=0 in Ω, with u=1 and ∂νu=0 on ∂Ω for k∈(31.967007261, 31.967007293), is constructed on a bounded simply connected noncircular domain with real-analytic Jordan boundary. This disproves Schiffer's conjecture and, via Green's identity (vanishing Fourier transform of the indicator function on the circle), also disproves the Pompeiu conjecture.

Conclusion: Both Schiffer's conjecture and the planar Pompeiu conjecture for bounded simply connected Lipschitz domains are false, as demonstrated by an explicit rigorous computational construction.

Abstract: The planar Pompeiu problem, originating in 1929, and the associated Schiffer conjecture are long-standing rigidity questions linking rigid-motion integral transforms and Fourier zero sets to overdetermined Neumann eigenvalue problems. We construct a bounded simply connected noncircular domain $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^2$ with real-analytic Jordan boundary and a nonconstant function $u$ such that $(Δ+k^2)u=0$ in $Ω$, $u=1,\partial_νu=0$ on $\partialΩ$ for some $k\in(31.967007261,31.967007293)$. Thus $u$ is a Neumann eigenfunction which is constant on the boundary, and $Ω$ is a counterexample to Schiffer's conjecture. Green's identity also gives $\widehat{\mathbf 1_Ω}(kω)=0$ $(ω\in\mathbb S^1)$, so $Ω$ fails the Pompeiu property and is also a counterexample to the planar Pompeiu conjecture for bounded simply connected Lipschitz domains. We obtain the domain as $Ω=φ(\mathbb{D})$, where $φ$ is a ten-fold symmetric conformal map close to an explicitly listed polynomial of degree $301$. On the unit disc, the analytic problem becomes a cubic operator equation on real coefficient spaces, $F(g,p)=g+|p|^2(1+Kg)=0$, where $K$, expressed in a disk-polynomial basis, is an explicit inverse of the Laplacian on the range compatible with zero Dirichlet and Neumann traces, and $p=kφ'$. Positivity of the disk-polynomial linearisation coefficients, sharp bounds for $K$, and monotone control of the infinite tails establish an a posteriori contraction near the listed polynomial in a weighted coefficient algebra, and hence an exact zero of $F$.

</details>


### [76] [The Riemann Problem for a 3x3 Generalized Chaplygin Gas System with Variable Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01580)
*Thomas Allen,Ella Kim,Jonathan Nunes,Rita Xing,Charis Tsikkou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Riemann problem for a 3x3 system of conservation laws with generalized Chaplygin pressure depending on a transported variable. It classifies Riemann solutions including singular delta-shock solutions, verifies them via Dirac delta distribution, compares with Nedeljkov's shadow-wave construction, applies the Dafermos entropy dissipation principle for admissibility, and constructs viscous profiles for isolated overcompressive delta-shocks using spherical blow-up techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and classify the Riemann solutions for a 3x3 system of conservation laws with generalized Chaplygin pressure that depends on an additional transported variable, particularly focusing on singular solutions and their physical admissibility, as well as establishing viscous profile realizations for these singular waves.

Method: The authors analyze the wave structure of the system, classify Riemann solutions, and use the Dirac delta framework to verify singular solutions in the distributional sense. They compare with Nedeljkov's shadow-wave construction, apply the Dafermos maximum entropy dissipation principle for admissibility, and construct viscous profiles via Dafermos regularization with spherical blow-up in three directional charts to prove heteroclinic orbit perturbation results.

Result: The paper provides a full classification of Riemann solutions (shocks, rarefactions, contact discontinuities, and singular delta-shocks), shows that the two complementary descriptions (Dirac delta and shadow-wave) agree, demonstrates the Dafermos principle selects physically relevant solutions, validates results with Lax-Friedrichs simulations, and proves that the isolated overcompressive delta-shock is realized as the zero-viscosity limit of self-similar Dafermos viscous profiles.

Conclusion: The generalized Chaplygin pressure system admits both classical and singular Riemann solutions, with the Dafermos entropy principle serving as an effective admissibility criterion. The constructed viscous profiles confirm the physical realizability of isolated overcompressive delta-shocks as zero-viscosity limits.

Abstract: We consider the Riemann problem for a 3x3 system of conservation laws with generalized Chaplygin pressure $p(ρ,v)=-\frac{A(v)}{ρ^α}$, $0<α\leq 1$, where the pressure depends on an additional transported variable. We analyze the system's wave structure and classify the Riemann solutions. Whenever classical solutions consisting of shocks, rarefaction waves, and contact discontinuities fail to exist, singular solutions arise. We verify that these satisfy the conservation laws in the distributional sense within the classical Dirac delta framework, and compare them with Nedeljkov's shadow-wave construction, giving two complementary descriptions of the same singular solution. We further study admissibility via the Dafermos maximum entropy dissipation principle, with several examples showing how it selects the physically relevant solution. Lax-Friedrichs simulations illustrate the Riemann wave patterns and provide a comparison with the analytical results.
  To construct viscous profiles for the isolated overcompressive $δ$-shock, we assume $α\in\mathbb{Q}\cap(0,1]$ and apply the Dafermos regularization together with a spherical blow-up. Working in three directional charts, we construct the reduced singular concatenation consisting of the left outer orbit, the middle orbit on the blown-up boundary, and the right outer orbit. We then prove that, for sufficiently small positive viscosity, this singular concatenation perturbs to a heteroclinic orbit. Consequently, the isolated overcompressive $δ$-shock is realized as the zero-viscosity limit of a family of self-similar Dafermos viscous profiles.

</details>


### [77] [Asymptotic Flocking Behavior in Particle and Kinetic Systems with biological Memory Term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01627)
*Yishuo Wang,Yawei Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a Cucker-Smale flocking model augmented with a biological memory term and finds that memory hinders flocking. The memory dynamics follow dh/dt = x + v - λh, combining positional, velocity, and exponentially decaying memory effects. At the microscopic level, flocking becomes conditional, requiring a polynomially bounded communication kernel and exponentially decaying memory deviation. At the mesoscopic level, flocking rates slow to exponential or algebraic decay, and the permissible range of communication strength β narrows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how biological memory affects collective flocking behavior, extending the classical Cucker-Smale model which does not account for memory effects in animal/agent motion.

Method: The authors introduce a memory variable h(t) into the Cucker-Smale model with dynamics dh/dt = x(t) + v(t) − λh(t), capturing path dependence, velocity dependence, and exponential memory decay. They analyze the system at both microscopic and mesoscopic (kinetic) levels, deriving conditions for flocking emergence and convergence rates.

Result: Flocking becomes harder with memory: at the microscopic level, flocking requires a communication kernel with polynomial lower bound and exponentially decaying memory deviation H(t). At the mesoscopic level, convergence rates are exponential or algebraic (slower than the memory-free case), and the admissible range of communication strength β shrinks.

Conclusion: The inclusion of biological memory makes flocking more difficult to achieve, manifesting in stricter conditions for flocking onset and slower convergence rates across both microscopic and mesoscopic scales.

Abstract: We investigate a Cucker-Smale type flocking model with a biological memory term and conclude that memory makes flocking harder. The model introduces $h(t)$ to describe memory, with dynamics $\frac{dh}{dt}=x(t)+v(t)-λh(t)$ reflecting path, velocity dependence and exponential decay. In microscopic level, the flocking phenomenon becomes conditional, requires communication kernel $ψ(t)$ has polynomial lower bound and memory deviation $H(t)$ decays exponentially. In mesoscopic level, flocking rates exhibit exponential or algebraic decay and the range of communication strength $β$ become smaller.

</details>


### [78] [Smooth Failure of Boundary Unique Continuation for Harmonic Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01683)
*Guolin Qin,Weicheng Zhan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs smooth harmonic functions in upper half-spaces with vanishing complete boundary jets on compact nowhere dense sets of positive measure, resolving a 1990 open problem by Bourgain and Wolff in the negative and disproving Nadirashvili's smooth unit-ball conjecture on boundary singular sets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the boundary unique-continuation problem left open by Bourgain and Wolff (1990) in the smooth category, and to disprove Nadirashvili's conjecture on boundary singular sets for smooth harmonic functions in the unit ball.

Method: The construction proceeds via a two-dimensional building block lifted cylindrically to higher dimensions. A hidden flexibility principle for nonlocal elliptic equations is uncovered: microscopic modifications control macroscopic exterior data through a quantitative correction mechanism for the half-Laplacian, iterated across scales to produce flat nonlocal Cauchy data on positive-measure sets. A Möbius-Kelvin transfer then yields the unit-ball counterexample in dimension three.

Result: For every n≥3, nonconstant smooth harmonic functions exist with vanishing complete boundary jets on compact nowhere dense sets of positive (n-1)-dimensional measure, with the exceptional set able to occupy an arbitrarily large proportion of a fixed boundary cube. This negatively resolves the smooth boundary unique-continuation problem and disproves Nadirashvili's conjecture.

Conclusion: Nonlocality exhibits a dual character: while long-range interactions create unique-continuation rigidity, they also provide the flexibility through which that rigidity fails in the smooth category. The paper thus fundamentally changes our understanding of boundary singular sets for harmonic functions.

Abstract: For every $n\geq 3$, we construct a nonconstant real-valued function $U\in C^\infty(\overline{\mathbb R^n_+})$, harmonic in the upper half-space, whose complete boundary jet vanishes on a compact nowhere dense subset of $\partial\mathbb R^n_+$ of positive $(n-1)$-dimensional measure. The essential construction takes place in two dimensions and yields the case $n=3$; higher-dimensional examples follow by cylindrical lifting. In every dimension, the exceptional set may occupy an arbitrarily large proportion of a fixed boundary cube. This resolves, in the negative, the smooth case of the boundary unique-continuation problem left open by Bourgain and Wolff in 1990 \cite[p.~260]{BourgainWolff1990}. In dimension three, a Möbius--Kelvin transfer gives the corresponding counterexample in the unit ball. It disproves Nadirashvili's smooth unit-ball conjecture on boundary singular sets \cite[Conjecture~4, p.~232]{Nadirashvili1997} and, a fortiori, disproves the gradient-only formulation subsequently recorded by Logunov and Malinnikova \cite[Section~7.4]{LogunovMalinnikova2020} and by Lin \cite[Conjecture~3, pp.~15--16]{Lin2020Current}.
  The proof uncovers a hidden flexibility principle for nonlocal elliptic equations: microscopic modifications can exert macroscopic control over exterior data. A quantitative correction mechanism for the half-Laplacian, iterated across scales, produces flat nonlocal Cauchy data on a set of positive measure. Thus nonlocality has a striking dual character: the same long-range interaction that drives unique-continuation rigidity can also furnish the flexibility through which that rigidity fails in the smooth category.

</details>


### [79] [On Weighted Local Morrey-type and Complementary Local Morrey-Type Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01722)
*Amiran Gogatishvili,Yoshihiro Sawano,Tuğçe Ünver*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes necessary and sufficient conditions for continuous embeddings between weighted local Morrey-type spaces and weighted complementary local Morrey-type spaces, using a one-dimensional reduction approach that converts multidimensional problems into weighted inequalities for Cesàro and Copson function spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide complete characterizations of embeddings between weighted local Morrey-type spaces and their complementary counterparts, covering all finite parameter cases, which had not been fully resolved previously.

Method: The authors reduce multidimensional embedding problems to one-dimensional weighted inequalities involving Cesàro and Copson function spaces, then apply recent results on characterizations of embeddings between these spaces to obtain the desired embedding conditions.

Result: Complete characterizations are provided for the embeddings between weighted local Morrey-type spaces and from complementary local Morrey-type spaces into local Morrey-type spaces, covering all possible finite parameter values 0 < p1, p2, q1, q2 < ∞.

Conclusion: The one-dimensional reduction approach successfully yields full characterizations of these embedding problems, avoiding standard multidimensional techniques and leveraging existing results on Cesàro-Copson embeddings.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish necessary and sufficient conditions for continuous embeddings between weighted local Morrey-type spaces and weighted complementary local Morrey-type spaces. Rather than relying on standard multidimensional approaches, our methodology is based on a one-dimensional reduction. Specifically, we reduce the multidimensional embedding problems to corresponding weighted inequalities involving one-dimensional Cesàro and Copson function spaces. Applying this reduction alongside recent results in the characterization of the embeddings between Cesàro and Copson spaces, we provide complete characterizations for the embeddings \[ \LM_{p_1,q_1}(v_1, w_1) \hookrightarrow \LM_{p_2,q_2}(v_2, w_2)\] and \[\dual\LM_{p_1,q_1}(v_1, w_1) \hookrightarrow \LM_{p_2,q_2}(v_2, w_2).\] Our results cover all possible finite cases of the parameters $0 < p_1, p_2, q_1, q_2 < \infty$.

</details>


### [80] [The Burgers' Equation in the Hermite-Sobolev Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01806)
*Suprio Bhar,Rajeev Bhaskaran,Barun Sarkar*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence and uniqueness of local-in-time solutions to the viscous Burgers' equation in R^d when the initial condition lies in a Hermite-Sobolev space of index p (for suitable non-negative integers p). It also shows that if the initial condition is in Schwartz space, the solution remains in Schwartz space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish well-posedness (existence and uniqueness) of solutions to the viscous Burgers' equation in a broader class of function spaces, specifically Hermite-Sobolev spaces, and to provide regularity results showing preservation of Schwartz space regularity.

Method: The approach involves analyzing the viscous Burgers' equation with initial data in Hermite-Sobolev spaces, using analytical techniques to show existence and uniqueness of local-in-time solutions, along with a regularity result for Schwartz space initial data.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of local-in-time solutions are established when the initial condition lies in Hermite-Sobolev space of appropriate index p. Additionally, regularity is preserved: if the initial condition belongs to Schwartz space, the solution also belongs to Schwartz space.

Conclusion: The viscous Burgers' equation is well-posed locally in time in Hermite-Sobolev spaces for suitable non-negative integer indices, and Schwartz space regularity is preserved by the solution.

Abstract: In this paper, we show existence and uniqueness of solutions to the viscous Burgers' equation in $\mathbb{R}^d$, when the initial condition $u_0$ is in Hermite-Sobolev space of index $p$, for suitable non-negative integers $p$. Our solutions are local in time. We also have a regularity result, viz. if $u_0$ belongs to Schwartz space, then so does the solution.

</details>


### [81] [Exponential mixing for Korteweg-de Vries equation with localized noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01863)
*Yuxuan Chen,Shengquan Xiang,Zhifei Zhang,Jia-Cheng Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes exponential mixing for the randomly forced and weakly damped KdV equation on the torus in L², using bounded, localized, degenerate-in-high-frequencies noise, via a probabilistic framework, nonlinear smoothing through normal form transformations, and stabilization by localized forcing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To prove exponential mixing (strong ergodicity) for a stochastically forced dispersive PDE—the weakly damped KdV equation—under degenerate noise conditions, advancing the understanding of the connection between asymptotic compactness, control theory, and ergodicity for randomly forced dispersive systems.

Method: Combines a general probabilistic framework from prior work with nonlinear smoothing for KdV and its linearization achieved via normal form transformations, alongside a stabilization argument using localized forcing to establish exponential mixing in L²(T).

Result: Exponential mixing is established for the randomly forced and weakly damped KdV equation in L²(T) under bounded, localized, degenerate noise in high frequencies.
