Conclusion: The result contributes to a series of works linking asymptotic compactness, control theory, and ergodicity/mixing for randomly forced dispersive PDEs, demonstrating that exponential mixing holds even with degenerate noise structures.

Abstract: We establish exponential mixing for the randomly forced and weakly damped KdV equation in $L^2(\mathbb{T})$. The noise is bounded, localized, and degenerate in high frequencies. Our proof relies on a general probabilistic framework in [11,33], nonlinear smoothing for KdV and its linearization via normal form transformation, and stabilization of the system by localized force. This paper continues a series of works connecting asymptotic compactness, control theory, and ergodicity and mixing for randomly forced dispersive PDEs.

</details>


### [82] [Generalized solution and Weak-Strong uniqueness for a barotropic Euler-Riesz system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01872)
*Nilasis Chaudhuri*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the Euler–Riesz system on the torus in 2D and 3D—compressible Euler equations with barotropic pressure coupled to a repulsive nonlocal Riesz potential force. By recasting the nonlocal interaction via the Caffarelli–Silvestre extension into a local augmented problem, the authors define global-in-time dissipative solutions for arbitrarily large finite-energy data and prove weak (measure-valued)–strong uniqueness for all β∈(0,2) and γ>1 using a relative energy argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Euler–Riesz system couples compressible Euler dynamics with a nonlocal repulsive force via a Riesz kernel, which is mathematically challenging due to its nonlocal nature and lack of sufficient regularity theory. The authors aim to establish a robust solution framework for arbitrarily large data and guarantee uniqueness against strong solutions, despite the nonlocal coupling and possible defect measures.

Method: The nonlocal Riesz interaction is reformulated as a local identity in one extra spatial variable using the Caffarelli–Silvestre extension, treating the force as the trace of a local stress tensor. The coercivity of the total energy for the repulsive kernel enables a notion of global dissipative solution. Uniqueness is proven via an adapted relative energy inequality that controls the difference between weak and strong solutions.

Result: Weak (measure-valued)–strong uniqueness is established for every order β∈(0,2) and every γ>1 independently: on any interval where a strong solution exists, every dissipative solution with the same initial data coincides with it, and all defect measures vanish.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous dissipative solution framework for the Euler–Riesz system with arbitrarily large finite-energy data, and proves strong uniqueness against strong solutions for the full range of parameters, demonstrating the power of the Caffarelli–Silvestre localization together with relative energy methods.

Abstract: We study the Euler--Riesz system on the torus $\mathbb T^d$, $d=2,3$: the compressible Euler equations with barotropic pressure $p(\varrho)=a\varrho^γ$ ($γ>1$, $a>0$), coupled to a repulsive nonlocal force ($\approx \varrho \nabla_x K \ast \varrho$) with Riesz kernel $K(x)\propto|x|^{β-d}$ of order $β\in(0,2)$. Since $K$ is the kernel of the inverse fractional Laplacian $(-Δ)^{-β/2}$, we recast the force through the Caffarelli--Silvestre extension as the trace of a local stress tensor, replacing the nonlocal interaction by a local identity in one extra variable. For a repulsive kernel the total energy is coercive, and we use this to introduce a notion of global-in-time \emph{dissipative solution} for arbitrarily large finite-energy data. Our main result is weak (measure-valued)--strong uniqueness, for every order $β\in(0,2)$ and every $γ>1$ independently: on any interval on which a strong solution exists, every dissipative solution with the same initial data coincides with it and all defects vanish. The proof rests on a suitable adaptation of relative energy.

</details>


### [83] [The uniqueness and non-uniqueness of solutions to the even dual Minkowski problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01912)
*Xiong Ge,Yang Kaiwen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Minkowski problem for even dual curvature measures, proving non-uniqueness of solutions for q>n (any dimension n≥2) and uniqueness for 0<q<n specifically in the case n=2. The results hinge on a newly established logarithmic Brunn-Minkowski inequality for dual quermassintegrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve questions of uniqueness and non-uniqueness for solutions to the Minkowski problem involving even dual curvature measures, which are fundamental geometric measure problems in convex geometry.

Method: The paper establishes a logarithmic Brunn-Minkowski inequality for dual quermassintegrals and uses it to derive the uniqueness and non-uniqueness results for the Minkowski problem across different parameter ranges.

Result: Non-uniqueness is proven for the case q>n in all dimensions n≥2, while uniqueness is proven for 0<q<n when n=2.

Conclusion: The uniqueness/non-uniqueness behavior of the Minkowski problem for even dual curvature measures is fully characterized for the specified parameter ranges, governed by the logarithmic Brunn-Minkowski inequality for dual quermassintegrals.

Abstract: For solutions to the Minkowski problem of the even dual curvature measures $\widetilde{C}_q$ in $\mathbb{R}^n$, $n\ge 2$, we prove the non-uniqueness for $q>n$ and $n\ge 2$, and the uniqueness for $0<q<n$ and $n=2$. These results are governed by our established logarithmic Brunn-Minkowski inequality for dual quermassintegrals $\widetilde V_q$.

</details>


### [84] [Unified theory for regularity persistence of vortex patch boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02033)
*Marc Magaña*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a unified local theory for persistence of Sobolev regularity of vortex patch boundaries in 2D active scalar equations with radial convolution kernels, covering 2D Euler, generalized SQG (0<β<1), and quasi-geostrophic shallow water equations. Under natural assumptions on the kernel, the authors prove local existence of unique H³ solutions (and H² solutions under stronger integrability) in contour dynamics, combining Sobolev energy estimates with quantitative arc-chord condition control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to provide a unified framework for studying the local well-posedness of vortex patch boundary regularity across a broad family of active scalar equations, rather than treating each equation (Euler, gSQG, QGSW) separately. This unifies and generalizes existing results in the literature.

Method: The proof combines two key ingredients: (1) Sobolev energy estimates for the contour dynamics equation to track the H³ (or H²) norm of the patch boundary, and (2) quantitative control of the arc-chord quantity, which prevents the boundary from self-intersecting. The method relies on natural assumptions on the kernel K: smoothness, integrability near the origin, monotonicity, and polynomial growth.

Result: Under natural assumptions on the kernel, if the initial boundary is in H³(T) and satisfies the arc-chord condition, there exists a unique local solution in C([0,T];H³(T)). Under a stronger integrability condition on the kernel, local existence of H² solutions is also obtained.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a unified local well-posedness theory for vortex patch boundary regularity applicable to multiple important equations in fluid dynamics, extending and consolidating prior results through a general kernel framework.

Abstract: We establish a unified local theory for the persistence of Sobolev regularity of vortex patch boundaries in a family of two-dimensional active scalar equations with radial convolution kernels $K(|x-y|)$. The class includes the 2D Euler equation, the generalized SQG equation in the locally integrable range $0<β<1$, and the quasi-geostrophic shallow water equation. Under natural assumptions on $K$ (smoothness, integrability near the origin, monotonicity, and polynomial growth), we prove that if the initial boundary belongs to $H^3(\mathbb T)$ and satisfies the arc-chord condition, then the contour dynamics equation admits a unique local solution in $C([0,T];H^3(\mathbb T))$. Under a stronger integrability condition on the kernel, we also obtain local existence of $H^2$ solutions. The proof combines Sobolev energy estimates for the contour equation with quantitative control of the arc-chord quantity.

</details>


### [85] [Balance between degenerate elliptic operators and coercive Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02198)
*Isabeau Birindelli,Giulio Galise,Hitoshi Ishii*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies boundary value problems for fully nonlinear degenerate elliptic equations of the form -λ_i(D²u)+|Du|^p+γu=f(x) in bounded domains, analyzing existence/nonexistence of solutions under Dirichlet and boundary blow-up conditions, and the asymptotic behavior as γ→0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to understand how the interplay between the degeneracy of the operator (governed by the i-th eigenvalue of the Hessian) and the superlinear growth of the Hamiltonian term |Du|^p affects the solvability and asymptotic behavior of these equations, revealing distinctly different phenomena depending on which term dominates and on the value of i.

Method: The paper employs a priori Lipschitz estimates as a key technical tool to analyze existence and nonexistence of solutions, and to characterize the asymptotic behavior as the parameter γ tends to zero. The analysis distinguishes between the case i=N (full Laplacian-like) versus i<N (degenerate cases).

Result: Key findings include: (1) The ergodic dichotomy (typical of mean-field game type problems) only occurs when i=N; (2) For i<N, new phenomena arise—under mild conditions, solutions that blow up even at a single point do not exist; (3) For i<N, conditions on the size of f must be imposed to guarantee existence of solutions to the Dirichlet problem with homogeneous boundary condition.

Conclusion: The study reveals that the combination of operator degeneracy (i<N) and superlinear Hamiltonian growth fundamentally changes the nature of the problem compared to the fully nondegenerate case (i=N), leading to new existence thresholds and nonexistence phenomena that depend critically on the eigenvalue index i.

Abstract: For $p>1$, we consider the boundary value problem for fully nonlinear degenerate elliptic equations $-λ_i(D^2u)+|Du|^p+γu=f(x)$ in bounded domains with Dirichlet or boundary blow-up conditions; here $λ_i(D^2u)$ denotes the $i$-th eigenvalue of the Hessian. We study existence and nonexistence of solutions together with the asymptotic behaviour of the solutions when $γ$ goes to zero. A priori Lipschitz estimates play an important role. The interplay between the operator's degeneracy and the superlinear growth of the Hamiltonian gives rise to phenomena that are very different depending on which of the two terms dominates, e.g. the ergodic dichotomy takes place only when $i=N$, while new phenomena arise for $i<N$ in which case, under mild conditions, solutions that blow up even in just one point do not exist, and conditions on the size of $f$ must be imposed for the existence of solutions to the Dirichlet problem with homogeneous boundary condition.

</details>


### [86] [The narrow escape problem in arbitrary dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02212)
*Louis Carillo,Tony Lelièvre,Thomas Normand,Gabriel Stoltz,Urbain Vaes*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the narrow escape problem (estimating exit time and position of a Brownian particle from a domain with small reflecting-boundary holes) in general domains of any dimension ≥2 using quasi-stationary distribution theory for metastability. They derive asymptotic expansions for the mean exit time and exit position law as hole sizes shrink to zero, validated by dedicated Monte Carlo simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The narrow escape problem serves as a canonical model for entropic metastability in biological and chemical systems. The goal is to rigorously determine exit time and position statistics for Brownian particles in general domains with small escape holes, extending previous analyses to arbitrary dimensions and domain geometries.

Method: The authors employ the quasi-stationary distribution (QSD) approach to metastability, which allows them to rigorously characterize the exit behavior. They derive asymptotic expansions for the mean exit time and the distribution of the exit position in the limit of vanishing hole sizes, and verify their analytical results with dedicated Monte Carlo numerical simulations.

Result: They obtain asymptotic expansions for both the mean exit time and the law of the exit position in general domains of any dimension ≥ 2 as the hole sizes approach zero. Numerical simulations using Monte Carlo techniques confirm the analytical predictions.

Conclusion: The quasi-stationary distribution framework provides a rigorous and general approach to the narrow escape problem in arbitrary dimensions and domain geometries, yielding accurate asymptotic predictions validated numerically.

Abstract: The narrow escape problem is a prototypical example for studying entropic metastability, motivated by the analysis of biological and chemical systems. The problem concerns the determination of the exit time and position of a Brownian particle trapped in a domain with a reflecting boundary pierced by narrow holes. Our goal is to investigate this problem in a general domain in any dimension (greater than or equal to two), using the quasi-stationary distribution approach to metastability. In particular, we derive the asymptotic expansion of the mean exit time and the law of the exit position in the limit where the hole sizes tend to zero. Our analytical predictions are illustrated by numerical simulations, using dedicated Monte Carlo techniques.

</details>


### [87] [Linear non-divergence parabolic equations in non-cylindrical space-time sets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02221)
*Luan Hoang,Akif Ibragimov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes second-order linear parabolic equations in non-divergence form on general non-cylindrical space-time domains where spatial cross-sections may grow unboundedly as time tends to infinity. The authors derive decay rates (exponential, power, and intermediate) for homogeneous problems and all-time/asymptotic estimates for inhomogeneous problems, using subtle properties of space-time sets and an adaptive iteration scheme for the Growth Lemma with non-constant time steps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand long-time behavior of solutions to parabolic equations on non-cylindrical domains where the spatial geometry evolves over time and can become unbounded, which generalizes classical results beyond fixed (cylindrical) domains.

Method: The approach exploits the geometry of general space-time sets and employs an iteration scheme to bootstrap the Growth Lemma. The iteration uses non-constant time steps that are adapted to the growth of the spatial diameter of the time cross-sections.

Result: Exponential, power, and intermediate decay rates are established for homogeneous problems depending on the scenario, and all-time plus asymptotic (as t→∞) estimates are proven for inhomogeneous problems in terms of boundary data and forcing functions.

Conclusion: The paper provides a comprehensive framework for quantifying solution decay and estimates in non-cylindrical parabolic settings with growing spatial domains, offering results that interpolate between exponential and power-law regimes.

Abstract: We study linear parabolic equations of the second order in non-divergence form in a general set which is non-cylindrical with respect to the spatial and time variables. The restriction of the set on any bounded time interval is bounded, but the spatial diameter of each fixed-time cross section can be unbounded as time tends to infinity. For homogeneous problems, we obtain exponential, power and other intermediate decaying rates for the solutions in different scenarios. For inhomogeneous problems, we obtain all-time and asymptotic, as time tend to infinity, estimates for the solutions in terms of the data on the parabolic boundary and forcing functions. The analysis requires subtle properties of general space-time sets and an iteration scheme to bootstrap the Growth Lemma. The time steps for such an iteration need not be constant and are adapted to the growth of the diameter.

</details>


### [88] [The critical exponent for the three-dimensional semilinear wave equation with strong damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02278)
*Wenhui Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper resolves an open problem from 2014 by determining the critical exponent for the 3D semilinear wave equation with strong damping. The threshold is p = 7/3, with global solutions for p > 7/3 and finite-time blow-up for 1 < p ≤ 7/3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The critical exponent for the three-dimensional semilinear wave equation with strong damping was an open problem posed in 2014. The paper aims to settle this question definitively.

Method: The authors use positivity of the full velocity fundamental solution combined with a dimension-descent formula to obtain a positive half-line kernel, replacing the finite propagation property unavailable for strongly damped waves. This enables a nonlinear lower-bound parabolic iteration without radial symmetry or pointwise sign assumptions on initial data. At the critical power, a refined slicing argument on moving shells converts borderline logarithmic gain into the growth required for blow-up.

Result: The critical exponent is determined to be p_crit = 7/3. For p > 7/3, sufficiently small data yield global-in-time solutions, while for 1 < p ≤ 7/3, arbitrarily small smooth compactly supported data cause finite-time blow-up.

Conclusion: The paper conclusively resolves the open problem by establishing the critical exponent 7/3 through novel analytical techniques that handle the absence of finite propagation speed for strongly damped waves.

Abstract: In this manuscript, we determine the critical exponent for the three-dimensional semilinear wave equation with strong damping and thereby resolve an open problem posed in 2014. The threshold for the power nonlinearity $|u|^p$ is
  \begin{align*}
  p=p_{\mathrm{crit}}=\frac{7}{3}.
  \end{align*}
  Sufficiently small data generate global in-time solutions for $p>\frac{7}{3}$, whereas there exist arbitrarily small smooth compactly supported data whose solutions blow up in finite time for $1<p\leqslant\frac{7}{3}$. Positivity of the full velocity fundamental solution, combined with a dimension-descent formula, yields a positive half-line kernel and replaces the finite propagation property unavailable for strongly damped waves. This leads to a nonlinear lower-bound parabolic iteration without radial symmetry or pointwise sign assumptions on the initial data. At the critical power, a refined slicing argument on moving shells converts the borderline logarithmic gain into the growth required for blow-up.

</details>


### [89] [Equilibration versus Localization in a Diffusion-Relaxation System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02307)
*Aseel AlNajjar,Hoyoun Kim,Athanasios Tzavaras*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper rigorously analyzes a diffusion-relaxation system to characterize the transition between equilibration (when diffusion dominates) and localization (when effective diffusion is weak). Convergence to constant equilibria is established via energy dissipation and invariant-region estimates, while the existence of self-similar localizing solutions is proven via geometric singular perturbation theory applied to an autonomous dynamical system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While equilibration versus localization behaviors have been studied through formal asymptotics and linearized stability analysis for various models, rigorous understanding of the associated nonlinear phenomena remains limited, particularly in higher dimensions. This paper aims to provide a rigorous characterization of this transition.

Method: In the equilibration regime, the authors exploit an energy dissipation structure and invariant-region estimates to establish convergence toward constant equilibria. In the localization regime, they study self-similar solutions, transforming the existence problem into constructing a heteroclinic orbit for an autonomous dynamical system, and apply geometric singular perturbation theory to prove existence.

Result: Rigorous convergence results are established for the equilibration regime, and the existence of self-similar profiles associated with localizing solutions is proven through heteroclinic orbit construction and geometric singular perturbation theory. The analysis provides a rigorous characterization of the equilibration-to-localization transition.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous nonlinear analysis that bridges the gap left by formal asymptotics and linearized stability studies, offering a complete characterization of the diffusion-relaxation system's behavior across the equilibration-localization transition.

Abstract: We consider a diffusion-relaxation system and investigate the conditions on parameters leading to equilibration versus localization. When the diffusion is dominant, solutions converge toward homogeneous equilibria. By contrast, when the effective diffusion is weak, localization emerges. Such behaviors have been studied for various models through formal asymptotic arguments and linearized stability analysis, but rigorous understanding of the associated nonlinear phenomena remains limited, particularly in higher dimensions. In the equilibration regime, we establish convergence toward constant equilibria by exploiting an energy dissipation structure and invariant-region estimates. In the localization regime, we study self-similar solutions and transform the problem of their existence into an autonomous dynamical system. The existence of self-similar profiles associated to localizing solutions is reduced to the construction of a heteroclinic orbit for an autonomous dynamical system. Their existence is obtained through an application of geometric singular perturbation theory. Our analysis provides a rigorous characterization of the transition from equilibration to localization.

</details>


### [90] [Strong ill-posedness for the MHD system in the supercritical regime: inviscid and viscous](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02330)
*Zhenyu Wan,Weikui Ye,Zhaoyang Yin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes norm inflation (strong ill-posedness) for the 3D incompressible MHD equations in supercritical Sobolev spaces, both with and without Laplacian dissipation. A novel "Magnetic-solo ansatz" shows that for the ideal MHD system, norm inflation occurs exclusively in the magnetic field while the velocity remains bounded, revealing asymmetric ill-posedness driven solely by the magnetic field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Cauchy problem for 3D incompressible MHD equations is locally well-posed in subcritical Sobolev spaces, but the supercritical regime remains largely open. This paper aims to fill this gap by establishing ill-posedness in the supercritical regularity range.

Method: The authors introduce a novel geometric construction called the "Magnetic-solo ansatz," which is used to demonstrate norm inflation in supercritical Sobolev spaces. This construction is applied to both the MHD system with Laplacian dissipation and the ideal (dissipation-free) MHD system. For the ideal case, the ansatz is designed so that norm inflation occurs only in the magnetic field b in H^s (0 < s < 5/2), while the velocity field u stays uniformly bounded.

Result: Norm inflation is established for the 3D incompressible MHD equations in supercritical Sobolev spaces for both the dissipative and ideal cases. Remarkably, for the ideal system, the ill-posedness manifests exclusively through the magnetic field, with the velocity field remaining uniformly bounded in H^s. This demonstrates asymmetric supercritical ill-posedness driven purely by the magnetic field dynamics.

Conclusion: The findings fill a significant gap in the supercritical regularity theory for incompressible MHD equations. The asymmetric behavior (magnetic-only ill-posedness) highlights the essential role of the magnetic field in the breakdown of well-posedness and reveals distinct mechanisms governing fluid versus magnetic dynamics in the supercritical regime.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the Cauchy problem for the 3D incompressible magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) equations in supercritical Sobolev spaces. It is well known that the system is locally well-posed in subcritical Sobolev spaces, whereas the supercritical regime remains largely open. In this work, we establish norm inflation for the incompressible MHD equations, both with and without Laplacian dissipation, in supercritical Sobolev spaces, thereby revealing strong ill-posedness of the system at this regularity level. A distinctive feature of our approach is the introduction of a novel geometric construction, termed the ``Magnetic-solo ansatz'', through which, for the ideal MHD system, norm inflation occurs exclusively in the magnetic field $b$ in $H^s$ with $0<s<\frac{5}{2}$, while the $H^s$-norm of the velocity field $u$ remains uniformly bounded. This asymmetric behavior shows that supercritical ill-posedness can be driven exclusively by the magnetic field, highlighting its essential role in the breakdown of well-posedness. Our findings fill a significant gap in the supercritical regularity theory for incompressible MHD and shed light on the distinct mechanisms governing the fluid and magnetic dynamics.

</details>


### [91] [Pointwise endpoint limits for nonlocal operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02340)
*Dinghuai Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes pointwise endpoint limits for nonlocal operators using two approaches: normalized integrals and distributional limits based on weighted weak-type norms. These limits yield various classical formulas (BBM, Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova, BSSVY, Gu-Yung) with higher-order and mean-oscillation variants. The authors also investigate endpoint limits for fractional powers from semigroups and approximation processes, derive sharp endpoint estimates in harmonic analysis, and resolve an open problem from Domínguez and Milman by proving sharp pointwise limits and two-sided iterated weak-type estimates at p=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a unified framework for pointwise endpoint limits of nonlocal operators that generalizes and connects several important classical results (Bourgain-Brezis-Mironescu, Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova, etc.), and to resolve an open endpoint case (p=1) raised by Domínguez and Milman in product spaces.

Method: Two types of pointwise endpoint limits are established: (1) limits based on normalized integrals, and (2) distributional limits using weighted weak-type norms. These are applied to derive classical formulas and their higher-order/mean-oscillation variants. The framework is extended to fractional powers generated by semigroups and approximation processes, yielding sharp strong and weak endpoint estimates.

Result: The limits produce pointwise versions of BBM, Maz'ya-Shaposhnikova, Brezis-Seeger-Van Schaftingen-Yung, and Gu-Yung formulas with higher-order and mean-oscillation variants. Sharp strong and weak endpoint estimates are derived for operators in harmonic analysis. The paper proves a sharp pointwise limit in the parameter variable and obtains two-sided iterated weak-type estimates at p=1, resolving the open endpoint case left by Domínguez and Milman.

Conclusion: The paper provides a general and systematic theory of pointwise endpoint limits for nonlocal operators, unifying and extending multiple classical results while also resolving a previously open problem at the endpoint p=1 in product spaces.

Abstract: We establish two pointwise endpoint limits for nonlocal operators: one based on normalized integrals and a distributional limit based on weighted weak-type norms. As applications, the limits yield pointwise Bourgain--Brezis--Mironescu, Maz'ya--Shaposhnikova, Brezis--Seeger--Van Schaftingen--Yung and Gu--Yung formulas, together with higher-order and mean-oscillation variants. We then investigate endpoint limits for fractional powers generated by semigroups and for approximation processes, and derive sharp strong and weak endpoint estimates for operators in harmonic analysis. Finally, while Domínguez and Milman obtained weak-type estimates on product spaces for $p>1$ and left the endpoint $p=1$ open (see [Adv.~Math.~411 (2022), Paper~No.~108774, p.~22]), we prove a sharp pointwise limit in the parameter variable and obtain two--sided iterated weak-type estimates at $p=1$.

</details>


### [92] [Spectrum analysis and optimal time decay rates of a kinetic-fluid-Poisson system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02377)
*Junhao Chen,Hailiang Li,Mingying Zhong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Cauchy problem for the coupled Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck/Navier-Stokes-Poisson (VPFP/NSP) system, providing a detailed spectrum analysis of the linearized system and establishing global existence of solutions with optimal time decay rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unlike the individual VPFP and NSP systems, the coupled system gains new coupling mechanisms through friction force and self-consistent Poisson equation, leading to a fundamentally different spectrum structure that requires careful analysis.

Method: The authors introduce a suitable norm to capture the forcing effect from the Poisson equation and perform a detailed spectrum analysis of the linearized system around a global equilibrium, followed by nonlinear analysis for global existence.

Result: The low-frequency spectrum exhibits a pair of acoustic branches with propagation speed sqrt((γ+1)/2) and two diffusive branches. Global existence is established with optimal time decay rate (1+t)^(-3/4), and faster rate (1+t)^(-5/4) for electric field and relative velocity.

Conclusion: The two coupling mechanisms produce an essentially different spectrum structure from individual systems, restoring classical acoustic wave propagation, and the framework can be extended to related kinetic-fluid models coupled via friction and self-consistent fields.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the Cauchy problem for the Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck/Navier-Stokes-Poisson (VPFP/NSP) system, which couples the VPFP system with the compressible NSP system through a friction force dependent on the relative velocity and a self-consistent Poisson equation. Motivated by the spectrum analysis for the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann (VPB) system, we introduce a suitable norm to capture the effect of the forcing induced by the Poisson equation and give a detailed spectrum analysis of the linearized system around a global equilibrium. Our results show that the two coupling mechanisms lead to an essentially different spectrum structure of the coupled system from those of the individual VPFP and NSP systems. More precisely, the low-frequency spectrum contains a pair of acoustic branches with the propagation speed $\sqrt{\frac{γ+1}{2}}~(γ\ge1)$ and two diffusive branches, thereby restoring the usual acoustic wave propagation of classical compressible fluids. Moreover, we establish the global existence of the solution to the nonlinear system and obtain the optimal time decay rate $(1+t)^{-\frac34}$, with a faster rate $(1+t)^{-\frac54}$ for the electric field and relative velocity. The present analysis also provides a useful framework for studying related kinetic-fluid models coupled through friction and self-consistent fields.

</details>


### [93] [Admissibility criteria for convex integration fan solutions and contact discontinuities in the Euler equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02482)
*Heiko Gimperlein*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes entropy and action rates for convex integration solutions to the isentropic Euler equations relative to piecewise constant fan subsolutions, showing these rates depend only on the fan and can be decomposed into kinetic and internal energy mismatches. This characterization is applied to Krupa-Székelyhidi solutions for Riemann data with planar contact discontinuities, demonstrating via certified exact-arithmetic computations that both Dafermos' entropy rate and action rate criteria prefer the convex integration solutions over the classical self-similar solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how selection criteria (entropy rate vs. action rate) compare when evaluating non-classical (convex integration) solutions to the isentropic Euler equations, particularly in cases where classical solutions exist (like contact discontinuities).

Method: Explicit computation of entropy and action rates for convex integration solutions associated with piecewise constant fan subsolutions, decomposing these rates into kinetic and internal energy mismatch terms. Certified exact-arithmetic computations are used to evaluate pairwise comparisons of these criteria.

Result: The entropy and action rates depend only on the fan subsolution, not the specific convex integration solution. The rates decompose into kinetic-energy and internal-energy mismatch components. For Riemann data with planar contact discontinuities, both Dafermos' entropy rate criterion and action rate criteria prefer the convex integration solutions over the classical self-similar solution.

Conclusion: Both selection criteria agree in preferring convex integration solutions over the classical contact discontinuity solution, indicating potential non-uniqueness even when classical solutions exist, and providing a framework for comparing selection criteria in the isentropic Euler system.

Abstract: For a general piecewise constant fan subsolution to the isentropic Euler equations, the explicit computation of the entropy and action rates of any associated convex integration solution, relative to a common reference solution, shows that these rates depend only upon the fan. Moreover, these explicit expressions may be decomposed into a kinetic-energy mismatch and an internal-energy mismatch from which we characterize agreement of Dafermos' entropy rate criterion with action rate criteria. We apply this to the solutions constructed by Krupa and Székelyhidi for Riemann data whose classical solution is a planar contact discontinuity. Certified exact-arithmetic computations show that, in pairwise comparison, both criteria prefer the convex integration solutions to the classical self-similar solution.

</details>


### [94] [Convergence Rate of Birkhoff Average for Toral Quasi-Periodic Rotations and Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02489)
*Son N. T. Tu,Jianlu Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes quantitative Denjoy-Koksma type estimates for higher-dimensional quasi-periodic torus rotations, providing convergence rates for Birkhoff averages of observables with Besov-type regularities, which yield sharp convergence rates for Hölder observables and improved results for homogenization of Hamilton-Jacobi equations and invariant measure regularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors seek to improve quantitative estimates on the discrepancy between Birkhoff averages (time averages) and spatial averages for quasi-periodic torus rotations in higher dimensions, particularly for non-smooth (Hölder continuous) observables, filling a gap in existing theory and enabling sharper results in applications.

Method: The paper develops quantitative Denjoy-Koksma type estimates using Diophantine frequency vectors and observables with various Besov-type regularities, combined with suitable Sobolev embeddings to derive convergence rates for Hölder continuous observables.

Result: The estimates yield the sharpest currently available convergence rates for Hölder continuous observables, substantially improved quantitative homogenization results for Hamilton-Jacobi equations in spatially quasi-periodic settings, and nearly optimal statistical regularity estimates for invariant measures under perturbations.

Conclusion: The paper provides a significant advancement in quantitative ergodic theory for quasi-periodic systems, with direct improvements in PDE homogenization and perturbation theory of invariant measures.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish quantitative Denjoy--Koksma type estimates for higher-dimensional quasi-periodic torus rotations. For Diophantine frequency vectors, we establish quantitative estimates on the discrepancy between Birkhoff averages and spatial averages for observables with various Besov-type regularities. By means of suitable Sobolev embeddings, these estimates yield, to the best of our knowledge, the sharpest currently available convergence rates for Hölder continuous observables. As applications, we obtain substantially improved quantitative homogenization results for Hamilton--Jacobi equations in spatially quasi-periodic settings, as well as nearly optimal statistical regularity estimates for invariant measures under perturbations.

</details>


### [95] [On countable subsets of solutions of nonlinear higher-order ODEs and elliptic PDEs with indefinite operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02532)
*Pablo Alvarez-Caudevilla,Jonathan D. Ev ans,Victor A. Galaktionov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs countable families of solutions for higher-order nonlinear ODEs and elliptic PDEs with non-coercive, indefinite operators arising from reaction-diffusion, thin film, and dynamical systems theories. Using a gluing/matching argument with exponentially decaying tails, the authors obtain countable subsets of homoclinic solutions of arbitrary complexity for equations with non-odd nonlinearities, where classical Lusternik-Schnirel'man variational theory does not apply.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses higher-order nonlinear ODEs and elliptic PDEs with non-coercive, indefinite operators where standard variational tools like Lusternik-Schnirel'man theory fail because the nonlinearities are non-odd (non-symmetric). This creates a gap in existence theory that the authors aim to fill by developing an alternative approach based on gluing/matching of solutions.

Method: The authors use a gluing/matching argument. They identify two periodic orbits Γ_max and Γ_min, then construct two countable families F_1,2 of positively dominant solutions. By gluing together arbitrary finite samples from these periodic orbits using exponentially decaying tails, they build homoclinic solutions in R^4 of arbitrary complexity. This is applied to a broad class of equations: classic and quasilinear degenerate ODEs (fourth and sixth order) with both smooth and non-smooth nonlinearities, including Hamiltonian cases from dynamical systems theory, as well as related polyharmonic elliptic PDEs in R^N.

Result: The paper demonstrates that a large class of higher-order ODEs and elliptic PDEs with non-odd, non-coercive nonlinearities admit countable families of solutions. Two basic countable families F_1,2 of positively dominant solutions are shown to exist, connected with two periodic orbits. Moreover, gluing finite samples from these periodic orbits yields a countable subset of homoclinics of arbitrary complexity, effectively providing a rich solution structure even in cases where classical variational methods are inapplicable.

Conclusion: The gluing/matching approach provides a robust framework for establishing existence of countable families of solutions for higher-order nonlinear ODEs and elliptic PDEs with non-coercive, non-odd nonlinearities where L-S genus/category variational theory fails. This yields homoclinic solutions of arbitrary complexity in the dynamical systems sense, extending the theory to a significantly broader class of equations from reaction-diffusion, thin film, and DS theories.

Abstract: Countable subsets of solutions of higher-order nonlinear ODEs and elliptic PDEs with indefinite non-coercive operators from the reaction-diffusion, thin film and dynamical system (DS) theories are obtained via a gluing/matching argument. In particular we study some classic and quasilinear degenerate ODEs in $\mathbb{R}$, with boundary conditions at infinity $F(\infty)=0$, with non-odd nonlinearities such as
  $$ \begin{matrix} F^{(4)} =-F+F^2,\, \,\, F^{(4)}=-F+F^2{\rm e}^{F-1}, \, (|F''|F'')''=-F + F^2, \\ F^{(4)} =-|F|F+F^2, \,\,\,F^{(4)} =-F^3+F^4, \,\,\, F^{(6)}=F-F^2, \end{matrix} $$ etc, as well as equations with odd and non-smooth nonlinearities like
  $$F^{(4)}=-F+F^3, \,F^{(4)}=-F-(|F|F-F)'', \,\, F^{(4)}=- \frac F{\sqrt{|F|}}-(F^3-F)'',\,\mbox{etc.}$$ Some of these ODEs are Hamiltonian and were studied in detail in the DS theory. On the basis of nonlinear operators, elliptic PDEs and variational theory, related polyharmonic elliptic equations in $\mathbb{R}^N$, $F(\infty)=0$, such as $$Δ^2 F=-F+F^2, \quad Δ^2 F=-F -Δ(F^2-F), \quad Δ^3 F=F-F^2, \quad \mbox{etc.};$$ are also shown to admit countable families of solutions. For such equations with non-odd functionals associated Lusternik--Schnirel'man (L--S) genus/category variational theory guaranteeing existence of a sequence of critical points does not apply. These ODEs and elliptic PDEs (e.g., in the radial setting) are shown to admit at least two basic countable families ${\mathcal F}_{1,2}$ of positively dominant solutions connected with two periodic orbits $Γ_{\rm max/min}$. Patterns obtained by gluing together via exponentially decaying tails of arbitrary finite samples from $Γ$'s form a countable subset of homoclinics in $\mathbb{R}^4$ of an arbitrary complexity.

</details>


### [96] [Failure of Fixed-Profile Modified Scattering at the Pure $L^2$ Endpoint for the 1D Defocusing Cubic NLS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02534)
*Xi Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves that the standard fixed-profile modified-scattering ansatz fails at the unweighted L^2 endpoint for the 1D defocusing cubic NLS equation, by constructing a smooth, rapidly decaying initial datum with unbounded moment whose corrected Fourier profile has no strong L^2 limit. An inductive construction of disjoint smooth bumps, combined with explicit Zakharov-Shabat transfer matrix composition and Deift-Zhou phase amplification, generates high-frequency oscillations in the transmission coefficient logarithm that preclude convergence despite L^1∩L^2 convergence of partial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses a fundamental open question in dispersive PDE theory: whether the modified-scattering ansatz for the 1D cubic defocusing NLS equation holds at the unweighted L^2 endpoint. Prior work established modified scattering in weighted spaces or with additional regularity assumptions; this paper aims to determine whether the endpoint case (with only L^2-type control) admits a fixed, time-independent asymptotic profile.

Method: The construction uses an inductively chosen sum of disjoint smooth bumps to build the initial datum q_*. The Zakharov-Shabat transfer matrices are composed exactly, inserting high-frequency oscillations into the logarithm of the transmission coefficient. The Deift-Zhou phase's one-sided logarithmic operator amplifies insertions of size ε_n by a factor of order log X_n. Setting ε_n log X_n = κ (a constant) ensures uniform separation between consecutive smooth asymptotic profiles, even though the partial data converge in L^1∩L^2.

Result: The construction yields a real-valued datum q_* ∈ L^1 ∩ ⋂_{k≥0} H^k with x q_* ∉ L^2, of arbitrarily prescribed L^2 norm, for which the corrected Fourier profile has no strong L^2 limit. This definitively shows that the fixed-profile modified-scattering ansatz fails at the unweighted L^2 endpoint.

Conclusion: The obstruction to modified scattering at the L^2 endpoint is specific to a single time-independent profile. The result leaves open the possibility of adaptive or scale-dependent renormalizations that could still capture the long-time asymptotics, suggesting that more flexible frameworks may be needed beyond the standard fixed-profile ansatz.

Abstract: We prove that the standard fixed-profile modified-scattering ansatz fails at the unweighted $L^2$ endpoint for the one-dimensional defocusing cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation. More precisely, there exists a real-valued datum $$q_* \in L^1(\mathbb{R}) \cap \bigcap_{k \geq 0} H^k(\mathbb{R}), \quad x q_* \notin L^2(\mathbb{R})$$ for which the corrected Fourier profile has no strong $L^2$ limit. The $L^2$ norm of the datum may be prescribed arbitrarily. The construction is an inductively chosen sum of disjoint smooth bumps. Exact composition of the Zakharov--Shabat transfer matrices inserts a high-frequency oscillation into the logarithm of the transmission coefficient, while the one-sided logarithmic operator in the Deift--Zhou phase amplifies an insertion of size $ε_n$ by a factor of order $\log X_n$. Choosing $ε_n\log X_n=κ$ produces a uniform separation between consecutive smooth asymptotic profiles even though the partial data converge in $L^1\cap L^2$. The obstruction is specific to a single time-independent profile and leaves open adaptive or scale-dependent renormalizations.

</details>


### [97] [On the regularity theory for mixed local and nonlocal weighted quasilinear elliptic equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02557)
*Sanjit Biswas,Prashanta Garain*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a comprehensive local regularity theory for mixed local and nonlocal degenerate p-Laplace equations with Muckenhoupt A_p-weight degeneracy and general right-hand sides, covering local boundedness, lower semicontinuity, weak Harnack inequalities, Harnack inequalities, and local Hölder continuity. This is the first systematic regularity theory for such weighted mixed local-nonlocal equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a comprehensive local regularity theory for mixed local and nonlocal degenerate p-Laplace equations with Muckenhoupt A_p-weights, a setting where both local and nonlocal operators may degenerate simultaneously, extending prior work to a highly nonuniform weighted framework with general right-hand sides.

Method: The approach combines weighted analytic techniques with the De Giorgi-Nash-Moser iteration method, adapted to the mixed local-nonlocal setting.

Result: The paper establishes local boundedness and lower semicontinuity of weak subsolutions, weak Harnack inequalities for weak supersolutions, Harnack inequalities, and local Hölder continuity of weak solutions. Results are new even for homogeneous linear equations (p=2) under the natural assumption w in A_2.

Conclusion: This provides the first systematic regularity theory for mixed local and nonlocal equations with Muckenhoupt weights, substantially extending existing regularity theory to a degenerate weighted framework with general right-hand sides.

Abstract: We investigate a broad class of mixed local and nonlocal degenerate $p$-Laplace equations with general right-hand sides. The degeneracy is governed by Muckenhoupt $A_p$-weights, yielding a highly nonuniform elliptic framework in which both the local and nonlocal operators may degenerate simultaneously. We establish a comprehensive local regularity theory, including local boundedness and lower semicontinuity of weak subsolutions, weak Harnack inequalities for weak supersolutions, Harnack inequalities, and local Hölder continuity of weak solutions. Our approach combines weighted analytic techniques with the De Giorgi--Nash--Moser iteration method, adapted to the mixed local--nonlocal setting. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first systematic regularity theory for mixed local and nonlocal equations with Muckenhoupt weights. In particular, our results are new even for homogeneous linear equations ($p=2$) under the natural assumption $w\in A_2$, and therefore substantially extend the existing regularity theory for mixed local--nonlocal equations to a degenerate weighted framework with general right-hand sides.

</details>


### [98] [Talbot effect for the periodic Benjamin--Ono equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02567)
*Xi Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the Talbot effect for the periodic Benjamin-Ono equation with rough initial data, using Gérard-Kappeler-Topalov's smoothing theorem for Tao's gauge transform to reduce singularity analysis to an explicit quadratic gauge profile. They obtain rational-time gauge-Hardy representations for BV data, and for Talbot-admissible data (including finite-jump piecewise smooth BV data like the square wave), a finite sum of logarithmic and jump kernels up to continuous remainder. At irrational times they prove continuity under a Diophantine condition and exhibit a Liouville obstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand the Talbot effect (fractalization/curiosities in the evolution) for the periodic Benjamin-Ono equation with rough (low-regularity) initial data, and to provide a structural characterization consistent with prior numerical observations.

Method: The analysis leverages the smoothing theorem of Gérard-Kappeler-Topalov for Tao's gauge transform, reducing the singularity analysis to an explicit quadratic gauge profile. They derive rational-time gauge-Hardy representations and classify Talbot-admissible data via finite edge singularities in the initial gauge profile, yielding decompositions into logarithmic kernels and jump kernels plus continuous remainders.

Result: General bounded-variation data admit a rational-time gauge-Hardy representation. Talbot-admissible data (including finite-jump, piecewise smooth BV data such as the square wave) yield finite sums of logarithmic and one-jump kernels plus a continuous remainder. At irrational times, continuity holds under a finite Diophantine type condition, but a Liouville obstruction example shows the one-sided gauge profile need not be continuous at all irrational times.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous structural characterization of the Talbot effect for the periodic Benjamin-Ono equation with rough data, qualitatively consistent with numerical profiles by Alama Bronsard-Laurens, resolving the singularity structure at rational times and clarifying the dichotomy between Diophantine and Liouville behavior at irrational times.

Abstract: We study the Talbot effect for the periodic Benjamin--Ono equation with rough initial data. Using the smoothing theorem of Gérard--Kappeler--Topalov for Tao's gauge transform, we reduce the singularity analysis to an explicit quadratic gauge profile. For general bounded-variation data we obtain a rational-time gauge-Hardy representation. For a natural subclass of Talbot-admissible data, whose initial gauge profile has only finitely many edge singularities, this representation becomes a finite sum of logarithmic kernels and one-jump kernels, up to a continuous remainder. We show that finite-jump, piecewise smooth BV data are Talbot-admissible; in particular, this applies to the square wave. At irrational times, we prove continuity under a finite Diophantine type condition, and we also exhibit a Liouville obstruction showing that the corresponding one-sided gauge profile need not be continuous at all irrational times. The results give a rigorous structural result that is qualitatively consistent with the numerical profiles of Alama Bronsard--Laurens.

</details>


### [99] [An autonomous Lipschitz fast dynamo on the three-torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02586)
*Lukas Niebel*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs an explicit divergence-free, time-independent velocity field on the 3-torus that serves as a fast dynamo for the kinematic induction equation, achieving exponential magnetic field growth with a uniform rate independent of magnetic diffusivity, while the underlying particle flow exhibits only mild (at most linear) stretching growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The problem of fast dynamos—whether a velocity field can amplify magnetic fields exponentially at a rate independent of (or growing slowly with) the magnetic diffusivity—is a fundamental open question in magnetohydrodynamics. Previous constructions either lacked rigorous proof of the dynamo property or relied on extremely rough velocity fields. The authors aim to construct a velocity field that is a genuine fast dynamo while maintaining relatively gentle dynamical properties of the underlying flow.

Method: The authors construct a single velocity field u on the flat 3-torus T^3 that is real-valued, divergence-free, and belongs to W^{1,∞}. They analyze the spectral properties of the kinematic induction operator for small positive magnetic diffusivity, and prove a uniform lower bound on the real part of an eigenvalue. They simultaneously verify that the associated particle flow and its inverse have Lipschitz constants growing at most linearly, zero topological entropy for every time map, and zero exponential growth rate for the ideal induction group in operator norm.

Result: For every sufficiently small positive magnetic diffusivity, the induction operator admits an eigenvalue whose real part is bounded below by a positive constant independent of the diffusivity. There exists a non-zero divergence-free solution whose L^2-norm grows exactly exponentially with a uniformly positive rate. The velocity field is differentiable everywhere, smooth away from a single circle, but not C^1—strikingly, this fast dynamo is achieved despite the flow having zero topological entropy and at most linear stretching of material line elements.

Conclusion: This construction demonstrates that fast dynamo action is possible even when the underlying Lagrangian flow is not chaotic (in the sense of positive Lyapunov exponents or positive topological entropy), and that a single explicit velocity field can serve as a fast dynamo. The mixing constraint for fast dynamos is therefore weaker than previously suspected.

Abstract: We construct a single real-valued, divergence-free, time-independent velocity field $u\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^3;\mathbb{R}^3) $ that is a fast dynamo for the kinematic induction equation on the flat three-torus. For every sufficiently small positive magnetic diffusivity, the corresponding induction operator has an eigenvalue whose real part is bounded below by a positive constant independent of the diffusivity. For each such diffusivity, there is a non-zero real-valued, divergence-free solution of the induction equation whose $\mathrm{L}^2 $-norm obeys an exact exponential growth law with a uniformly positive rate. For the same velocity field, the particle flow and its inverse have Lipschitz constants growing at most linearly in time, every time map has zero topological entropy, and the ideal induction group has zero exponential growth rate in operator norm. The velocity is differentiable everywhere and smooth away from a single circle, but is not $\mathrm{C}^1 $.

</details>
