### [138] [Performance of radio-based detection to operational monitoring M5+ class solar flares](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02199)
*Julia van Ravenswaaij,Ines Wilms,Ivan Ricardo,Michaela Brchnelova*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: The paper investigates using ground-based radio observations (from the Radio Solar Telescope Network) as an alternative to space-based soft X-ray flux for monitoring major solar flares (M5+ class). An elastic net regularized logistic regression model trained on data from March 2023 to March 2025 achieved 53% precision and 65% recall for flare detection, with signals appearing 3-4 minutes before the M5 threshold, particularly at higher frequencies (8800 MHz).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Early detection of major solar flares is critical for defense operations since flares can disturb radar and radio systems. However, the standard monitoring method (soft X-ray flux) requires space-based measurements, whose availability is itself dependent on space weather conditions—creating a circular vulnerability. This motivates exploring ground-based alternatives.

Method: The authors used datasets from the GOES-16 satellite and the Radio Solar Telescope Network covering March 2023 to March 2025. They trained an elastic net regularized logistic regression model, optimized via grid search, with class weighting incorporated to handle class imbalance in the flare/non-flare data.

Result: Higher radio frequencies (especially 8800 MHz) showed reasonable capability in monitoring and predicting major flares, achieving 53% precision and 65% recall for flare events (roughly one third of flares undetected), with predictive signals appearing on average 3-4 minutes before the M5 threshold is exceeded.

Conclusion: Super high frequency radio measurements can serve as a viable alternative method for monitoring major solar flaring activity, reducing dependence on space-based observations.

Abstract: Early detection of major solar flares is critical for defense operations due to their potential to disturb radar and radio systems. Typically, soft X-ray flux is used to monitor and classify solar flares, but since this flux has to be measured in space, it means that its availability itself is dependent on space weather conditions. For this reason, in this paper, we investigated the feasibility of using ground radio observations to monitor major (M5+ class) solar flares. We made use of datasets from the GOES-16 satellite and the Radio Solar Telescope Network in the time range between March 2023 and March 2025. An elastic net regularized logistic regression model was trained on this data, optimized through a grid search and with incorporated class weighting for class imbalance. It was found that especially higher frequencies (8800 MHz) had a reasonable ability in monitoring and predicting major flares (precision and recall for flare events are 53% and 65%, respectively - implying that roughly one third of flares were not detected - with signals appearing, on average, 3 to 4 minutes before the M5 threshold is exceeded). Radio measurements at super high frequencies can thus serve as an alternative method to monitor major solar flaring activity.

</details>


<div id='math.DG'></div>

# math.DG [[Back]](#toc)

### [139] [Spectral determinants of the Bolza surface and the Klein quartic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01611)
*Victor Kalvin*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper derives closed-form explicit formulas for spectral determinants of two notable hyperbolic surfaces (the Bolza surface and Klein quartic) by expressing them multiplicatively in terms of determinants of singular quotient orbifolds, evaluated via the Polyakov anomaly formula and determinant formulas for conically singular spheres, and proves these surfaces are critical points of the spectral determinant on their Teichmüller spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain explicit, computable values of spectral determinants for important hyperbolic surfaces, which are difficult to compute in general, by exploiting their highly symmetric quasiplatonic structure.

Method: The determinant of each surface is decomposed multiplicatively into determinants of lower-genus singular orbifolds (genus zero and one). Elliptic factors are evaluated using the singular Polyakov anomaly formula applied to Belyi maps on CM elliptic curves (discriminants -8 and -7), while genus-zero factors use explicit determinant formulas for constant-curvature spheres with conical singularities. The relations are shown to hold fibrewise over equisymmetric deformation strata.

Result: Closed explicit formulas for the spectral determinants of the Bolza surface and Klein quartic are obtained, plus determinant and first-variation identities on deformation strata, and a general theorem that every compact quasiplatonic hyperbolic surface is a critical point of the spectral determinant on its Teichmüller space.

Conclusion: The spectral determinants of these highly symmetric surfaces can be computed explicitly via orbifold decomposition and anomaly formulas, and quasiplatonic surfaces are characterized as critical points of the spectral determinant functional.

Abstract: We obtain closed explicit formulas for the spectral determinants of the smooth hyperbolic Bolza surface and the Klein quartic. In each case, a multiplicative relation expresses the determinant of the surface in terms of determinants of singular quotient orbifolds of genera zero and one. The elliptic factors are evaluated by applying the singular Polyakov anomaly formula to explicit Belyi maps on CM elliptic curves of discriminants -8 and -7, while the genus-zero factors are evaluated by explicit determinant formulas for constant-curvature spheres with conical singularities. The same multiplicative relations hold fibrewise on the corresponding equisymmetric deformation strata and yield determinant and first-variation identities. We also prove that every compact quasiplatonic hyperbolic surface is a critical point of the spectral determinant on its Teichmüller space; in particular, this applies to the Bolza surface and the Klein quartic.

</details>


<div id='quant-ph'></div>

# quant-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [140] [Adaptive operator-generated subspaces for effective many-body Hamiltonians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00560)
*Ginanjar Utama,Hermawan Kresno Dipojono*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: A-CASE is an operator-generated Rayleigh-Ritz method that reconstructs quantum-chemical matrices from a shared Pauli-expectation bank, enabling adaptive subspace growth with overlap-aware local pencils, validated on linear H4 to FCI precision and benchmarked against ADAPT-VQE and generalized Krylov methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Electronic-structure workflows terminate in effective many-body Hamiltonians while quantum eigensolvers often start from hand-built qubit models, creating a gap. The authors aim to bridge this by providing an operator-generated, single-reference eigensolver that connects from interchange Hamiltonians to energies, correlations, and response.

Method: A-CASE uses a shared Pauli-expectation bank to reconstruct overlap, Hamiltonian, observable, and response matrices. It employs adaptive growth scores based on overlap-aware local pencils, rejects symmetry leakage or near-linear dependence, and uses a strict FCIDUMP adapter for active-space boundaries. A grouped bootstrap propagates finite-shot variability through the full analysis pipeline.

Result: For linear H4 in STO-3G with CAS(4e,4o), A-CASE matches FCI to 3.1e-15 Ha. At nine vectors it has 3.019 mHa error; with ADAPT-VQE reference this drops to 0.342 mHa. Fixed-reference uses one state prep vs ADAPT-VQE's ninety but measures ~10x more Pauli words. Exact fixed-angle ADAPT-GCIM gives 13.364 mHa at nearest size match, 10.674 mHa at iteration match. Fixed Krylov bases are generally more accurate and narrower but substantially less well conditioned.

Conclusion: The work establishes an executable path from interchange Hamiltonian to energies, correlations, and response, without claiming materials accuracy, favorable scaling, or quantum advantage.

Abstract: Electronic-structure and embedding workflows terminate in effective many-body Hamiltonians, whereas quantum eigensolver studies often start from hand-built qubit models. We present the Adaptive Clifford-Algebra Subspace Eigensolver (A-CASE), a single-reference, operator-generated Rayleigh--Ritz method. Overlap, Hamiltonian, observable, and response matrices are reconstructed from one shared Pauli-expectation bank, while adaptive growth scores overlap-aware local pencils and rejects symmetry leakage or near-linear dependence. A strict FCIDUMP adapter supplies the active-space boundary. For linear H$_4$ in STO-3G with CAS(4e,4o), the mapped sector agrees with independent determinant FCI to $3.1\times10^{-15}$ Ha. At a nine-vector budget A-CASE has a $3.019$ mHa error; replacing the determinant reference by a two-operator ADAPT-VQE state reduces it to $0.342$ mHa, without implying a matched total-cost advantage. Under a matched contract, the fixed-reference route uses one state preparation versus ADAPT-VQE's ninety but measures roughly an order of magnitude more Pauli words. Exact fixed-angle ADAPT-GCIM gives $13.364$ mHa at the nearest size match and $10.674$ mHa at the iteration match, with its transition-pair burden reported separately. Across a broader benchmark ladder, fixed Krylov bases are generally more accurate and often narrower but substantially less well conditioned. A grouped bootstrap propagates finite-shot variability through thresholding, diagonalization, root matching, spectral weights, susceptibility, and broadening; its bands are explicitly heuristic and conditional, not finite-sample confidence certificates. The work establishes an executable path from an interchange Hamiltonian to energies, correlations, and response, without claiming materials accuracy, favorable scaling, or quantum advantage.

</details>


### [141] [Spatial-Order Hierarchy of Time-Dependent Exchange-Correlation Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01483)
*Naoki Negishi*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper establishes a representability constraint linking the interaction and kinetic-correlation components of the exchange-correlation potential in time-dependent density-functional theory, based on off-diagonal expansions of the one-electron reduced density matrix relative to a TDHF reference. Higher-order off-diagonal structure activates the kinetic component, and neglecting it suppresses density broadening in correlated dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In exact time-dependent density-functional theory, the exchange-correlation potential separates into interaction and kinetic-correlation components, but the structural relationship between these two parts is unknown, hindering non-adiabatic functional construction.

Method: Derives a representability constraint by expanding the one-electron reduced density matrix in off-diagonal terms relative to a TDHF reference, and tests the consequences in a one-dimensional two-electron correlation quench by comparing the exact interaction component against calculations with the kinetic-correlation component set to zero.

Result: A non-zero linear off-diagonal term generates non-HF current density while the kinetic-correlation component remains HF representable; higher-order off-diagonal structure activates the kinetic component. Removing the kinetic component suppresses exact density broadening in a two-electron correlation quench.

Conclusion: The findings establish a hierarchy of density equations of motion and provide an exact constraint useful for constructing non-adiabatic functionals in time-dependent density-functional theory.

Abstract: Exact time-dependent density-functional theory separates the exchange-correlation potential into interaction and kinetic-correlation components, but the structural relation between them remains unknown. We establish a representability constraint based on the off-diagonal expansion of the one-electron reduced density matrix relative to a time-dependent Hartree-Fock (TDHF) reference. A non-zero linear term generates a non-HF current density while the kinetic-correlation component remains HF representable; higher-order off-diagonal structure activates the kinetic component. In a one-dimensional two-electron correlation quench, retaining the exact interaction component while setting the kinetic component to zero suppresses the density broadening of the exact evolution. These results establish a hierarchy of density equations of motion and provide an exact constraint for non-adiabatic functional construction.

</details>


### [142] [Thermalization of open quantum systems with pseudomodes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02517)
*R. Kevin Kessing,Thibaut Lacroix,Susana F. Huelga,Martin B. Plenio*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates under what conditions pseudomode models—which exactly describe quantum systems strongly coupled to baths—satisfy detailed balance and thermalize to the system's Gibbs state. The authors establish criteria for when detailed balance holds, explore how parameter choices can yield "weak" detailed balance over limited frequency ranges, and consider hybrid Hermitian/non-Hermitian pseudomode configurations that produce a flat effective-temperature profile.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pseudomode approaches exactly capture quantum systems strongly interacting with baths, but they typically do not thermalize to the Gibbs state. This conflicts with thermodynamic intuition, especially in the asymptotically weak coupling limit, and it is unclear under what conditions thermodynamic consistency can be achieved.

Method: The authors analyze conditions under which pseudomode models satisfy detailed balance, derive criteria for thermalization to the system Gibbs state, investigate how specific parameter choices can enforce "weak" detailed balance restricted to limited frequency ranges, and examine a combination of Hermitian and non-Hermitian pseudomodes to achieve a flat effective-temperature profile.

Result: The paper establishes concrete criteria for when pseudomode models satisfy detailed balance and thermalize to the Gibbs state, demonstrates how parameter choices can induce frequency-restricted "weak" detailed balance, and shows that combining Hermitian and non-Hermitian pseudomodes can yield a flat effective-temperature profile.

Conclusion: The results provide practical guidelines for constructing pseudomode models that maintain thermodynamic consistency, which is essential for applications requiring accurate thermalization behavior.

Abstract: Pseudomode approaches allow for an exact and unapproximated description of a quantum system interacting arbitrarily strongly with a bath. In general, a system coupled to pseudomodes will not thermalize to the system's Gibbs state: This is to be expected when the system-bath coupling is non-perturbative, but conflicts with common thermodynamic intuition when the system-bath coupling is asymptotically weak. We explore under which circumstances pseudomode models satisfy detailed balance (and subsequently thermalize to the system Gibbs state) and how specific choices of parameters can force "weak" detailed balance that is restricted to a limited frequency range. A combination of Hermitian and non-Hermitian pseudomodes that yields a flat effective-temperature profile is also considered. The results and criteria established here are relevant for the construction of pseudomode models in contexts where thermodynamic consistency is required.

</details>
